# v2 Local-First JSE Data App Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** Replace the MongoDB scraper with `jamstockex`, a single-user, standard-library-only Python 3.11+ app that keeps Jamaica Stock Exchange end-of-day data in a local `jse-data.json` and serves it read-only on `127.0.0.1` as a JSON API with Swagger docs, released as unsigned one-file executables and a `.pyz`.

**Architecture:**
- **One process:** `jamstockex` (or `jamstockex serve`) loads `jse-data.json`, serves an immutable in-memory snapshot through `http.server` on `127.0.0.1:8765`, and runs a background thread. Every `check_every_minutes` the thread compares clocks; when a refresh is due and allowed, it fetches only what is due (typically 2 requests per trading day), parses tables by header name, merges, saves atomically and swaps the snapshot.
- **Blocks:** a block (403/429/CloudFront page) is never retried or worked around. Stored data is still served, `/v2/status` says `blocked`, and the next automatic attempt waits for the next market close.
- **CLI:** `jamstockex refresh [--listings]` runs one refresh and prints a report. `--version`, `--data-dir`, `--port` and `--offline` are the only other options; there is no host option.

**Tech Stack:** Python 3.11+ standard library only (`tomllib`, `urllib.request`, `html.parser`, `http.server`, `dataclasses`, `decimal`, `importlib.resources`, `zipapp`); `pytest==8.3.3` (dev only); `pyinstaller==6.10.0` (build time only); vendored `swagger-ui-dist` 5.33.3; GitHub Actions.

**Spec:** `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-10-v2-local-app-design.md` (authoritative). The rules it cites from `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-09-v2-static-api-design.md` (§3.2 parsing, §3.3 listings due logic, §5 data shapes and conventions, §6.1–§6.2 failure classes and merge rules, §9 tests) are binding.

## Global Constraints

**Runtime and dependencies**
- `jamstockex/` is **Python 3.11+ standard library only**. `tests/test_stdlib_only.py` enforces it with an `ast` scan against `sys.stdlib_module_names`.
- The only dev dependency is `pytest==8.3.3` (`requirements-dev.txt`). `pyinstaller==6.10.0` is installed only inside `release.yml`. There is no `requirements.txt`.
- Local development uses `.venv`, created with `/opt/homebrew/bin/python3.12 -m venv --clear .venv`. CI covers 3.11 and 3.12.

**Behaviour towards the JSE site**
- **One honest User-Agent:** `jamstockex/2.0 (+https://github.com/sdclarkelab/jamstockex-scraper)`. **Never rotate or spoof it, never imitate a browser, and never retry or work around a block** (JSE T&C §8.2).
- **Error messages name the request path, never the host or URL.** They are shown in `/v2/status`.
- **No `jamstockex.com` URLs** in API responses, `openapi.json`, the docs page, README or NOTICE (JSE T&C §8.5.3). The source is named in plain text: `Data: Jamaica Stock Exchange Limited (jamstockex.com). All rights in the data belong to the JSE Group.`
- **Real JSE pages are never committed.** They live in the gitignored `tests/fixtures/real/`. Committed fixtures are synthetic, with fake companies.
- **Never contact the JSE site from tests, CI, or while implementing this plan.** The only live request is the user's own `jamstockex refresh` in the final task.

**Local only**
- The server binds `127.0.0.1` (hard-coded) and has **no host option**. A `Host` header other than `127.0.0.1:<port>` or `localhost:<port>` gets `403`.
- No CORS headers. Every response has `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff` and `Cache-Control: no-store`. `/docs` also has `Content-Security-Policy: default-src 'self'; img-src 'self' data:; style-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline'`.

**API output**
- GET only; API version path `/v2/`; release tag `v2.0.0` (the existing `v1.0` tag is untouched).
- Envelope `{"meta": {api_version, as_of, saved_at, stale, source, notice, license}, "data": …}`. `notice` is `Personal, noncommercial use only. Not financial advice.`; `license` is `Code: PolyForm Noncommercial 1.0.0`.
- JSON: minified (`separators=(",", ":")`), `sort_keys=True`, UTF-8, `snake_case` keys, ISO dates `YYYY-MM-DD`, UTC timestamps `YYYY-MM-DDTHH:MM:SSZ`, missing values `null` (never `0`), original letter case kept in values. Errors: `{"error": {"status": <code>, "message": "<text>"}}`.

**Tests**
- pytest, fully offline. Tests may open sockets on `127.0.0.1` only.

**Process**
- Work on branch `feat/v2-static-api` (it already exists and holds the spec). Stage files by explicit path; never `git add -A` (the untracked `jamstockex.log` is the user's).
- Every commit message ends with a blank line, then `Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>`. The `-m "…" -m "Co-Authored-By: …"` form used below does this.
- **Outward-facing actions need explicit user approval at the time:** `git push`, opening a PR, merging, and pushing tags. Push with `git -c credential.helper= -c credential.helper='!gh auth git-credential' push …`.
- No foreground `sleep` in commands; use `gh run watch` / `gh pr checks --watch` to wait.

**Decisions made while planning (the spec leaves these open):**
1. **Timezone:** `config.JAMAICA` is a fixed `UTC-05:00` offset named `America/Jamaica`. Jamaica has had no daylight saving time since 1983, so this matches the tz database, and it works on Windows, where `zoneinfo` has no data without the third-party `tzdata` package.
2. **`/v2/` index route:** the server also answers `/v2/` with the endpoint list. It is in `openapi.json`, and the route/OpenAPI parity test covers it.
3. **Data file format:** `jse-data.json` is indented, sorted UTF-8 JSON with `"format": 1`. It also stores `blocked_close` and `last_failure_at`, which the due logic needs. "Minified" applies to API responses.
4. **Numbers:** `Decimal` values are written as JSON numbers via `int`/`float`. This is exact for 15 or fewer significant digits, which covers all JSE prices.
5. **Signatures that refine the spec:** `store.save(state, path, *, now)`, `store.merge(state, result, *, today, now, settings, stats=None)`, `make_server(port, snapshot_provider, *, today=None)` and `Fetcher(source, opener=None, sleep=time.sleep)`. The extra parameters exist for tests.
6. **Methods and filters:** `HEAD` gets `405` like every other non-GET method. A filter given twice gets `400`.
7. **Aggregate `meta`:** `/v2/stocks` uses `as_of = quotes.as_of or listings.as_of` and `stale = quotes.stale or listings.stale`. `/v2/` and `/v2/status` are stale if any dataset is. `blocked` stays true until an attempt after the next close gets through.
8. **CLI:** `--data-dir`, `--port` and `--offline` may come before or after `serve`. `refresh` accepts only `--data-dir` and `--listings`. Exit codes: 0 ok; 1 a dataset failed or was blocked, or another refresh holds the lock; 2 config, data-file or port problem.
9. **Intel macOS runner:** `macos-15-intel`. If GitHub no longer offers it, apply the spec's §9 fallback: drop that matrix row and point Intel Mac users to the `.pyz` in the README.
10. **Holiday page for the real-page check:** 2026-10-19 (National Heroes Day, a Monday with no trading).

### Task 1: Manual gate: the user saves real JSE pages, and their structure is confirmed

**Why:** automated requests from the development machine are blocked, so the parsers can only be checked against pages the user saves from a normal browser visit. The synthetic fixtures in later tasks copy the layout the portfolio tracker verified in June 2026, and the live site may differ. This gate confirms the header names, the `instrument_code` format, that no page is paginated, and how a holiday is answered **before** any code depends on them. Nothing in this task sends a request to the JSE site; the user browses it themselves.

**Files:**
- Modify: `.gitignore` (append `tests/fixtures/real/`)
- User-created, gitignored, never committed: `tests/fixtures/real/*.html`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: nothing.
- Produces: the seven files below in `tests/fixtures/real/`, with exactly these names, which Task 17's `tests/test_parse_real.py` reads; a short findings note for the user's records (not committed).

- [ ] **Step 1: Ignore the real-pages folder before anything is saved there**

```bash
printf '\n# Real JSE pages saved by hand for local parser checks. Never commit them.\ntests/fixtures/real/\n' >> .gitignore
mkdir -p tests/fixtures/real
touch tests/fixtures/real/probe.html
git check-ignore tests/fixtures/real/probe.html
rm tests/fixtures/real/probe.html
```

Expected: `git check-ignore` prints `tests/fixtures/real/probe.html`. If it prints nothing, the ignore rule is wrong; fix it before going on.

- [ ] **Step 2: Commit the ignore rule**

```bash
git add .gitignore
git commit -m "chore: ignore real JSE pages saved for local parser checks" -m "Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>"
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Ask the user to save seven pages from their browser**

Send the user this message, word for word, and wait for their reply:

> Please open these seven pages in your normal browser and save each one with **Save Page As → Webpage, HTML Only** into `tests/fixtures/real/` in this repo. That folder is gitignored, and the pages are never committed. Type each path after the JSE site's address in your address bar:
>
> 1. `/listings/listed-companies/?market=31` (Main Market) → `listed-companies-31.html`
> 2. `/listings/listed-companies/?market=22` (Junior Market) → `listed-companies-22.html`
> 3. `/listings/listed-companies/?market=51` (Bond Market) → `listed-companies-51.html`
> 4. `/listings/listed-companies/?market=49` (USD Market) → `listed-companies-49.html`
> 5. `/trading/trade-quotes/?market=50&date=<a recent trading day, YYYY-MM-DD>` → `trade-quotes-trading-day.html`
> 6. `/trading/trade-quotes/?market=50&date=2026-10-19` (National Heroes Day, no trading) → `trade-quotes-holiday.html`
> 7. `/trading/corporate-actions/?instrumentCode=all-stocks&fromDate=2026-01-01&thruDate=<the date one year from today, YYYY-MM-DD>` → `corporate-actions.html`
>
> While you're on each page, please tell me: does it show page numbers, a "next page" link or a "load more" button? Does each listings page say which market it is, and do the names match the labels above? What does the holiday page show (an empty table, a message, or the previous day's prices)? Which trading day did you use for page 5?

- [ ] **Step 4: Inspect the saved pages (standard library only, offline)**

Run from the repo root once the user says the files are saved. The script reads only the local files:

```bash
/opt/homebrew/bin/python3.12 -I - <<'EOF'
import re
import sys
from html.parser import HTMLParser
from pathlib import Path
from urllib.parse import parse_qs, urlparse

REAL = Path("tests/fixtures/real")
MARKETS = {31: "Main Market", 22: "Junior Market", 51: "Bond Market", 49: "USD Market"}
FILES = [f"listed-companies-{code}.html" for code in MARKETS] + [
    "trade-quotes-trading-day.html", "trade-quotes-holiday.html", "corporate-actions.html"]
PAGINATION = re.compile(r"[?&](page|paged|offset|start)=\d|class=\"[^\"]*pagination|rel=\"next\"", re.I)


class Tables(HTMLParser):
    def __init__(self):
        super().__init__(convert_charrefs=True)
        self.tables, self.stack, self.cell, self.skip = [], [], None, 0

    def handle_starttag(self, tag, attrs):
        if tag in ("script", "style"):
            self.skip += 1
        elif tag == "table":
            self.stack.append([])
            self.tables.append(self.stack[-1])
        elif self.stack and tag == "tr":
            self.stack[-1].append([])
        elif self.stack and tag in ("td", "th"):
            if not self.stack[-1]:
                self.stack[-1].append([])
            self.cell = {"text": "", "th": tag == "th", "codes": []}
            self.stack[-1][-1].append(self.cell)
        elif self.cell is not None and tag == "a":
            code = parse_qs(urlparse(dict(attrs).get("href") or "").query).get("instrument")
            if code:
                self.cell["codes"].append(code[0])

    def handle_endtag(self, tag):
        if tag in ("script", "style"):
            self.skip = max(0, self.skip - 1)
        elif tag == "table" and self.stack:
            self.stack.pop()
            self.cell = None
        elif tag in ("td", "th"):
            self.cell = None

    def handle_data(self, data):
        if self.cell is not None and not self.skip:
            self.cell["text"] += data


def squash(text):
    return " ".join(text.split())


missing = [name for name in FILES if not (REAL / name).exists()]
for name in FILES:
    path = REAL / name
    print(f"== {name}")
    if not path.exists():
        print("   MISSING")
        continue
    html = path.read_text(encoding="utf-8", errors="replace")
    print(f"   bytes={len(html)}  cloudfront_block={'Request blocked' in html and 'cloudfront' in html.lower()}"
          f"  pagination_markers={sorted({m.group(0) for m in PAGINATION.finditer(html)})}")
    if name.startswith("listed-companies-"):
        label = MARKETS[int(name.split("-")[2].split(".")[0])]
        print(f"   mentions {label!r}: {label.lower() in html.lower()}")
    parser = Tables()
    parser.feed(html)
    for number, rows in enumerate(parser.tables):
        rows = [row for row in rows if row]
        header = [squash(cell["text"]) for cell in rows[0]] if rows else []
        body = [row for row in rows[1:] if any(not c["th"] for c in row) and any(squash(c["text"]) for c in row)]
        codes = [code for row in body for cell in row for code in cell["codes"]]
        print(f"   table {number}: header={header}")
        print(f"            data_rows={len(body)}  rows_with_instrument_link="
              f"{sum(any(c['codes'] for c in row) for row in body)}  sample_codes={list(dict.fromkeys(codes))[:5]}")
print()
print("all seven pages present" if not missing else f"STILL MISSING: {', '.join(missing)}")
sys.exit(1 if missing else 0)
EOF
```

Expected: one `==` block per file, then `all seven pages present`. If it prints `STILL MISSING: …`, ask the user for the named files and run it again. Each block shows the file size, whether it is a CloudFront block page, any pagination markers, whether a listings page mentions its market label, and, for every table, its header cells, its data-row count, how many rows carry an `?instrument=` link, and up to five sample instrument codes.

- [ ] **Step 5: Check the findings against the stop conditions**

Compare the output and the user's answers with this list:

| Check | Pass | Stop condition |
|---|---|---|
| Block page | `cloudfront_block=False` on every file | `True`: ask the user to save that page again from the browser |
| Pagination | `pagination_markers=[]`, and the user saw no page numbers, "next" link or "load more" | **STOP and redesign:** the app reads one page per request, so pagination would silently drop rows and change the request budget |
| Holiday | `trade-quotes-holiday.html` has a table whose header includes `Symbol` and a closing or last-traded price, with `data_rows=0` | **STOP and redesign** if the holiday page shows the previous session's prices (`Quote.date` would be wrong). Report to the user if it has no quotes table at all: the parser treats that as a layout change (`ParseError`), so the holiday rule would need a page-specific "no trades" marker |
| `instrument_code` | `sample_codes` look the same on the listings and the trade-quotes pages (same format, e.g. `SYMBOL-CUR`) | Report to the user if quotes rows carry no `?instrument=` link or the formats differ: quotes are keyed by that code |
| Headers | Each page has the headers the later tasks' aliases expect: listings `Security`/`Symbol` (plus `Currency`, `Sector`, `Type`, `Website`); quotes `Symbol` and `Closing Price ($)` or `Last Traded Price ($)` (plus `Price Change ($)`, `Volume`); indices `Index`/`Value`; corporate actions `Instrument`, `Action`, `Dividend Amount` (plus `Record Date`, `Ex-Date`, `Payment Date`) | A different header is not a stop: write it down. Task 17 adds it to the alias tuple in `jamstockex/parse.py` and changes the synthetic fixture's `<th>` |
| Corporate actions | A column says what kind of action each row is | **STOP and report** if there is no action-type column: `type` is required, and a default type must not be guessed |
| Market labels | Each listings page mentions its label (`mentions '…': True`), or the user confirms the names | Report to the user if a code shows a different market: the default `[markets]` in `config.py` would need changing |

If a STOP condition applies, stop this plan and tell the user what you found. Otherwise go on.

- [ ] **Step 6: Record the findings for the user (structure only, no JSE content)**

Tell the user, in a short message, the verified header lists for each page type, the `instrument_code` format with one example, the holiday behaviour, the pagination result and the trading day used for page 5. Keep a copy of the header lists for Task 17. Nothing is committed in this step.

### Task 2: Scaffold and config

**Why:** set up the package, the dev tooling and the settings every later module reads. Users need no config file; an optional `<data_dir>/config.toml` overrides any subset of the built-in defaults (spec §4). The root `__init__.py` goes now, because it makes pytest treat the repo root as a package.

**Files:**
- Delete: `__init__.py` (repo root)
- Modify: `.gitignore` (append `.venv/`)
- Create: `requirements-dev.txt`, `pytest.ini`, `jamstockex/__init__.py`, `jamstockex/config.py`
- Test: `tests/__init__.py` (empty), `tests/helpers.py`, `tests/test_config.py`, `tests/test_stdlib_only.py`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: nothing.
- Produces:
  - `jamstockex.__version__ = "2.0.0"`
  - `jamstockex.config`: `CONFIG_FILE = "config.toml"`; `JAMAICA: datetime.timezone` (fixed UTC-05:00, named `America/Jamaica`); `USER_AGENT: str`; `DEFAULTS: dict[str, dict[str, Any]]`; `class ConfigError(ValueError)` (message names the file and key)
  - `@dataclass(frozen=True) SourceConfig(base_url: str, user_agent: str, timeout_s: float, retries: int, delay_s: tuple[float, float])`
  - `@dataclass(frozen=True) RefreshConfig(close_time: dt.time, check_every_minutes: int, retry_after_failure_minutes: int, listings_every_days: int, corporate_actions_window: int, corporate_actions_forward: int, backfill_from: dt.date, max_listing_drop_pct: int)`
  - `@dataclass(frozen=True) Config(source: SourceConfig, markets: dict[int, str], combined_market: int, refresh: RefreshConfig, port: int)`
  - `load_config(data_dir: str | Path) -> Config`; `default_config() -> Config`
  - `tests.helpers`: `ROOT: Path` (repo root), `FIXTURES: Path` (`tests/fixtures`), `JSE_URL: re.Pattern` (matches any `http(s)://…jamstockex.com` URL), `fixture_html(name: str) -> str`

import re
from pathlib import Path

ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent
FIXTURES = Path(__file__).resolve().parent / "fixtures"
JSE_URL = re.compile(r"https?://[^\s\"'<>]*jamstockex\.com", re.IGNORECASE)

`tests/test_config.py`:

```python
import datetime as dt

import pytest

from jamstockex.config import JAMAICA, USER_AGENT, ConfigError, default_config, load_config


def write(tmp_path, text):
    (tmp_path / "config.toml").write_text(text, encoding="utf-8")
    return tmp_path


def test_defaults_need_no_config_file(tmp_path):
    config = load_config(tmp_path)
    assert config.source.base_url == "https://www.jamstockex.com"
    assert config.source.user_agent == "jamstockex/2.0 (+https://github.com/sdclarkelab/jamstockex-scraper)"
    assert config.source.user_agent == USER_AGENT
    assert config.source.timeout_s == 20.0
    assert config.source.retries == 3
    assert config.source.delay_s == (2.0, 5.0)
    assert config.markets == {31: "Main Market", 22: "Junior Market", 51: "Bond Market", 49: "USD Market"}
    assert config.combined_market == 50
    assert config.refresh.close_time == dt.time(17, 30)
    assert config.refresh.check_every_minutes == 15
    assert config.refresh.retry_after_failure_minutes == 60
    assert config.refresh.listings_every_days == 7
    assert config.refresh.corporate_actions_window == 30
    assert config.refresh.corporate_actions_forward == 365
    assert config.refresh.backfill_from == dt.date(2026, 1, 1)
    assert config.refresh.max_listing_drop_pct == 20
    assert config.port == 8765
    assert config == default_config()


def test_config_file_overrides_a_subset(tmp_path):
    config = load_config(write(tmp_path, """
[source]
retries = 5
base_url = "https://www.example.test/"

[refresh]
close_time = "16:45"
backfill_from = 2026-03-01

[server]
port = 9000
"""))
    assert config.source.retries == 5
    assert config.source.base_url == "https://www.example.test"
    assert config.source.timeout_s == 20.0  # untouched default
    assert config.refresh.close_time == dt.time(16, 45)
    assert config.refresh.backfill_from == dt.date(2026, 3, 1)
    assert config.refresh.listings_every_days == 7
    assert config.port == 9000


def test_markets_table_replaces_the_defaults(tmp_path):
    config = load_config(write(tmp_path, '[markets]\n31 = "Main Market"\n'))
    assert config.markets == {31: "Main Market"}


def test_backfill_from_accepts_a_string(tmp_path):
    config = load_config(write(tmp_path, '[refresh]\nbackfill_from = "2026-02-01"\n'))
    assert config.refresh.backfill_from == dt.date(2026, 2, 1)


@pytest.mark.parametrize("text, key", [
    ("[source]\nretries = 0", "source.retries"),
    ('[source]\nretries = "3"', "source.retries"),
    ("[source]\ndelay_s = [5, 2]", "source.delay_s"),
    ("[source]\ndelay_s = [1]", "source.delay_s"),
    ("[source]\ntimeout_s = 0", "source.timeout_s"),
    ('[source]\nbase_url = "http://www.example.test"', "source.base_url"),
    ('[source]\nuser_agent = " "', "source.user_agent"),
    ("[refresh]\nmax_listing_drop_pct = 120", "refresh.max_listing_drop_pct"),
    ("[refresh]\nlistings_every_days = 0", "refresh.listings_every_days"),
    ("[refresh]\ncheck_every_minutes = true", "refresh.check_every_minutes"),
    ('[refresh]\nclose_time = "25:00"', "refresh.close_time"),
    ('[refresh]\nbackfill_from = "soon"', "refresh.backfill_from"),
    ("[server]\nport = 70000", "server.port"),
    ('[markets]\nmain = "Main Market"', "markets.main"),
    ("[markets]", "markets"),
    ('[markets]\n31 = "Main Market"\n22 = "Main Market"', "markets"),
    ("[bogus]\nx = 1", "unknown section [bogus]"),
    ("[source]\ncolour = 1", "unknown key source.colour"),
])
def test_invalid_values_name_the_file_and_key(tmp_path, text, key):
    with pytest.raises(ConfigError) as caught:
        load_config(write(tmp_path, text))
    assert key in str(caught.value)
    assert str(tmp_path / "config.toml") in str(caught.value)


def test_bad_toml_names_the_file(tmp_path):
    with pytest.raises(ConfigError, match="invalid TOML"):
        load_config(write(tmp_path, "[source\n"))


def test_jamaica_is_utc_minus_five_all_year():
    for month in (1, 7):
        moment = dt.datetime(2026, month, 1, 12, tzinfo=JAMAICA)
        assert moment.utcoffset() == dt.timedelta(hours=-5)
```

`tests/test_stdlib_only.py`:

```python
"""jamstockex/ must import nothing outside the standard library (spec §3.1)."""

import ast
import sys

from tests.helpers import ROOT


def imported_modules(path):
    tree = ast.parse(path.read_text(encoding="utf-8"), filename=str(path))
    for node in ast.walk(tree):
        if isinstance(node, ast.Import):
            for alias in node.names:
                yield alias.name.split(".")[0]
        elif isinstance(node, ast.ImportFrom) and node.level == 0 and node.module:
            yield node.module.split(".")[0]


def test_package_imports_only_the_standard_library():
    files = sorted((ROOT / "jamstockex").rglob("*.py"))
    assert files, "no package files found"
    outside = {
        f"{path.relative_to(ROOT)}: {name}"
        for path in files
        for name in imported_modules(path)
        if name != "jamstockex" and name not in sys.stdlib_module_names and name != "__future__"
    }
    assert outside == set()
```

`jamstockex/__init__.py`:

```python
"""Local-first Jamaica Stock Exchange end-of-day data, served on 127.0.0.1."""

__version__ = "2.0.0"
```

`jamstockex/config.py`:

```python
"""Settings: built-in defaults, optionally overridden by <data_dir>/config.toml.

No config file is needed. A config.toml may override any subset of the keys in
DEFAULTS; a [markets] table replaces the default markets as a whole, so a
market can be removed. Unknown sections or keys are errors, so a typo can't be
silently ignored.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import copy
import datetime as dt
import re
import tomllib
from dataclasses import dataclass
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Any

CONFIG_FILE = "config.toml"

# America/Jamaica has been UTC-05:00 all year since 1983 (no daylight saving time). A fixed
# offset gives the same answers as the tz database, which Windows doesn't ship and which the
# standard library can't provide there without the third-party `tzdata` package.
JAMAICA = dt.timezone(dt.timedelta(hours=-5), "America/Jamaica")

USER_AGENT = "jamstockex/2.0 (+https://github.com/sdclarkelab/jamstockex-scraper)"

DEFAULTS: dict[str, dict[str, Any]] = {
    "source": {
        "base_url": "https://www.jamstockex.com",
        "user_agent": USER_AGENT,
        "timeout_s": 20,
        "retries": 3,
        "delay_s": [2, 5],
    },
    "markets": {"31": "Main Market", "22": "Junior Market", "51": "Bond Market", "49": "USD Market"},
    "quotes": {"combined_market": 50},
    "refresh": {
        "close_time": "17:30",
        "check_every_minutes": 15,
        "retry_after_failure_minutes": 60,
        "listings_every_days": 7,
        "corporate_actions_window": 30,
        "corporate_actions_forward": 365,
        "backfill_from": dt.date(2026, 1, 1),
        "max_listing_drop_pct": 20,
    },
    "server": {"port": 8765},
}


class ConfigError(ValueError):
    """config.toml is malformed or has an invalid value. The message names the file and key."""


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class SourceConfig:
    base_url: str
    user_agent: str
    timeout_s: float
    retries: int
    delay_s: tuple[float, float]


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class RefreshConfig:
    close_time: dt.time
    check_every_minutes: int
    retry_after_failure_minutes: int
    listings_every_days: int
    corporate_actions_window: int
    corporate_actions_forward: int
    backfill_from: dt.date
    max_listing_drop_pct: int


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Config:
    source: SourceConfig
    markets: dict[int, str]  # listed-companies market code -> label
    combined_market: int
    refresh: RefreshConfig
    port: int


def load_config(data_dir: str | Path) -> Config:
    """Defaults, overlaid with <data_dir>/config.toml when that file exists."""
    path = Path(data_dir) / CONFIG_FILE
    raw = copy.deepcopy(DEFAULTS)
    if path.exists():
        try:
            with path.open("rb") as handle:
                user = tomllib.load(handle)
        except tomllib.TOMLDecodeError as exc:
            raise ConfigError(f"{path}: invalid TOML: {exc}") from exc
        _overlay(raw, user, path)
    return _build(raw, path)


def default_config() -> Config:
    return _build(copy.deepcopy(DEFAULTS), Path("<built-in defaults>"))


def _overlay(raw: dict[str, dict[str, Any]], user: dict[str, Any], path: Path) -> None:
    for section, values in user.items():
        if section not in DEFAULTS:
            raise ConfigError(f"{path}: unknown section [{section}]")
        if not isinstance(values, dict):
            raise ConfigError(f"{path}: {section} must be a table ([{section}])")
        if section == "markets":
            raw["markets"] = dict(values)
            continue
        for key, value in values.items():
            if key not in DEFAULTS[section]:
                raise ConfigError(f"{path}: unknown key {section}.{key}")
            raw[section][key] = value


class _Reader:
    def __init__(self, raw: dict[str, dict[str, Any]], path: Path) -> None:
        self.raw = raw
        self.path = path

    def fail(self, key: str, problem: str) -> ConfigError:
        return ConfigError(f"{self.path}: {key}: {problem}")

    def int(self, section: str, key: str, minimum: int, maximum: int | None = None) -> int:
        value = self.raw[section][key]
        name = f"{section}.{key}"
        if isinstance(value, bool) or not isinstance(value, int):
            raise self.fail(name, f"must be an integer (got {value!r})")
        if value < minimum or (maximum is not None and value > maximum):
            limit = f">= {minimum}" if maximum is None else f"between {minimum} and {maximum}"
            raise self.fail(name, f"must be {limit} (got {value})")
        return value

    def number(self, section: str, key: str, value: Any = None) -> float:
        value = self.raw[section][key] if value is None else value
        if isinstance(value, bool) or not isinstance(value, (int, float)):
            raise self.fail(f"{section}.{key}", f"must be a number (got {value!r})")
        return float(value)

    def text(self, section: str, key: str) -> str:
        value = self.raw[section][key]
        if not isinstance(value, str) or not value.strip():
            raise self.fail(f"{section}.{key}", f"must be a non-empty string (got {value!r})")
        return value.strip()


def _build(raw: dict[str, dict[str, Any]], path: Path) -> Config:
    read = _Reader(raw, path)

    base_url = read.text("source", "base_url").rstrip("/")
    if not base_url.startswith("https://"):
        raise read.fail("source.base_url", "must start with https://")
    delay = raw["source"]["delay_s"]
    if not isinstance(delay, list) or len(delay) != 2:
        raise read.fail("source.delay_s", f"must be [low, high] (got {delay!r})")
    low, high = (read.number("source", "delay_s", value) for value in delay)
    if not 0 <= low <= high:
        raise read.fail("source.delay_s", "must be [low, high] with 0 <= low <= high")
    timeout = read.number("source", "timeout_s")
    if timeout <= 0:
        raise read.fail("source.timeout_s", "must be > 0")
    source = SourceConfig(
        base_url=base_url,
        user_agent=read.text("source", "user_agent"),
        timeout_s=timeout,
        retries=read.int("source", "retries", 1, 10),
        delay_s=(low, high),
    )

    markets: dict[int, str] = {}
    for code, label in raw["markets"].items():
        if not str(code).isdigit():
            raise read.fail(f"markets.{code}", "market codes must be whole numbers")
        if not isinstance(label, str) or not label.strip():
            raise read.fail(f"markets.{code}", "the label must be a non-empty string")
        markets[int(code)] = label.strip()
    if not markets:
        raise read.fail("markets", "must list at least one market")
    if len(set(markets.values())) != len(markets):
        raise read.fail("markets", "labels must be unique")

    close_raw = raw["refresh"]["close_time"]
    if isinstance(close_raw, dt.time):
        close_time = close_raw.replace(second=0, microsecond=0)
    elif isinstance(close_raw, str) and re.fullmatch(r"([01]\d|2[0-3]):[0-5]\d", close_raw):
        close_time = dt.time.fromisoformat(close_raw)
    else:
        raise read.fail("refresh.close_time", f'must be "HH:MM" (got {close_raw!r})')

    backfill = raw["refresh"]["backfill_from"]
    if isinstance(backfill, str):
        try:
            backfill = dt.date.fromisoformat(backfill)
        except ValueError:
            raise read.fail("refresh.backfill_from", f"must be a date like 2026-01-01 (got {backfill!r})") from None
    if isinstance(backfill, dt.datetime) or not isinstance(backfill, dt.date):
        raise read.fail("refresh.backfill_from", f"must be a date like 2026-01-01 (got {backfill!r})")

    refresh = RefreshConfig(
        close_time=close_time,
        check_every_minutes=read.int("refresh", "check_every_minutes", 1),
        retry_after_failure_minutes=read.int("refresh", "retry_after_failure_minutes", 1),
        listings_every_days=read.int("refresh", "listings_every_days", 1),
        corporate_actions_window=read.int("refresh", "corporate_actions_window", 0),
        corporate_actions_forward=read.int("refresh", "corporate_actions_forward", 0),
        backfill_from=backfill,
        max_listing_drop_pct=read.int("refresh", "max_listing_drop_pct", 0, 100),
    )

    return Config(
        source=source,
        markets=markets,
        combined_market=read.int("quotes", "combined_market", 1),
        refresh=refresh,
        port=read.int("server", "port", 1, 65535),
    )
```

### Task 3: Data models

**Why:** every other module passes these dataclasses around. They hold `Decimal` and `date` values and convert to and from the JSON shapes of the superseded spec §5 (`null`, never `0`; ISO dates).

**Files:**
- Create: `jamstockex/models.py`
- Test: `tests/test_models.py`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: nothing from earlier tasks.
- Produces:
  - `num(value: Decimal | None) -> int | float | None` (integral values become `int`); `dec(value: Any) -> Decimal | None`; `isodate(value: date | None) -> str | None`; `parse_iso(value: str | None) -> date | None`
  - `@dataclass Quote(date: date, close: Decimal | None, change: Decimal | None, change_pct: Decimal | None, volume: int | None)`
  - `@dataclass Company(instrument_code: str, symbol: str, name: str, market: str, currency: str | None = None, sector: str | None = None, type: str | None = None, website: str | None = None, listed: bool = True, listed_since: date | None = None, delisted_on: date | None = None, quote: Quote | None = None)`
  - `@dataclass Index(name: str, date: date, value: Decimal | None, change: Decimal | None, change_pct: Decimal | None, volume: int | None)`
  - `@dataclass(frozen=True) CorporateAction(symbol: str, type: str, amount: Decimal | None, currency: str | None, record_date: date | None, ex_date: date | None, payment_date: date | None, instrument_code: str | None = None)` with `key() -> tuple` = `(symbol, type, record_date, ex_date, payment_date, amount, currency)`
  - Every class has `to_json(self) -> dict[str, Any]` and `@classmethod from_json(cls, d: dict[str, Any])`

import datetime as dt
from decimal import Decimal

from jamstockex.models import Company, CorporateAction, Index, Quote, dec, num

DAY = dt.date(2026, 11, 9)


def test_num_and_dec():
    assert num(None) is None
    assert num(Decimal("12.00")) == 12 and isinstance(num(Decimal("12.00")), int)
    assert num(Decimal("0.7500")) == 0.75
    assert dec(None) is None
    assert dec(0.75) == Decimal("0.75")
    assert dec(12) == Decimal("12")
    assert dec(Decimal("1.5")) == Decimal("1.5")


def test_quote_round_trip():
    quote = Quote(date=DAY, close=Decimal("12.50"), change=Decimal("-0.25"), change_pct=Decimal("-1.96"), volume=48213)
    data = quote.to_json()
    assert data == {"date": "2026-11-09", "close": 12.5, "change": -0.25, "change_pct": -1.96, "volume": 48213}
    assert Quote.from_json(data) == quote


def test_company_round_trip_with_quote_and_nulls():
    company = Company(
        instrument_code="9001", symbol="ACME", name="Acme Holdings Limited", market="Main Market",
        currency="JMD", sector=None, type="Ordinary", website=None, listed=True,
        listed_since=DAY, delisted_on=None,
        quote=Quote(date=DAY, close=None, change=None, change_pct=None, volume=None),
    )
    data = company.to_json()
    assert data["sector"] is None and data["website"] is None
    assert data["listed_since"] == "2026-11-09"
    assert data["quote"] == {"date": "2026-11-09", "close": None, "change": None, "change_pct": None, "volume": None}
    assert set(data) == {
        "instrument_code", "symbol", "name", "market", "currency", "sector", "type",
        "website", "listed", "listed_since", "delisted_on", "quote",
    }
    assert Company.from_json(data) == company


def test_company_without_quote():
    company = Company(instrument_code="9002", symbol="BETA", name="Beta", market="USD Market")
    assert company.to_json()["quote"] is None
    assert Company.from_json(company.to_json()) == company


def test_index_round_trip():
    index = Index(name="JSE Main Index", date=DAY, value=Decimal("325456.78"),
                  change=Decimal("-1234.56"), change_pct=Decimal("-0.38"), volume=12345678)
    assert Index.from_json(index.to_json()) == index


def test_corporate_action_round_trip_and_key_ignores_scale_and_code():
    action = CorporateAction(symbol="ACME", type="dividend", amount=Decimal("0.7500"), currency="JMD",
                             record_date=dt.date(2026, 11, 20), ex_date=dt.date(2026, 11, 18),
                             payment_date=dt.date(2026, 12, 4), instrument_code=None)
    data = action.to_json()
    assert data == {
        "instrument_code": None, "symbol": "ACME", "type": "dividend", "amount": 0.75, "currency": "JMD",
        "record_date": "2026-11-20", "ex_date": "2026-11-18", "payment_date": "2026-12-04",
    }
    loaded = CorporateAction.from_json({**data, "instrument_code": "9001"})
    assert loaded.amount == Decimal("0.75")
    assert loaded.key() == action.key()

`jamstockex/models.py`:

```python
"""Plain dataclasses for JSE data, with JSON (de)serialisation."""

from __future__ import annotations

import datetime as dt
from dataclasses import dataclass
from decimal import Decimal
from typing import Any


def num(value: Decimal | None) -> int | float | None:
    """Decimal -> JSON number. Integral values become ints (12, not 12.0)."""
    if value is None:
        return None
    if value == value.to_integral_value():
        return int(value)
    return float(value)


def dec(value: Any) -> Decimal | None:
    if value is None:
        return None
    if isinstance(value, Decimal):
        return value
    return Decimal(str(value))


def isodate(value: dt.date | None) -> str | None:
    return None if value is None else value.isoformat()


def parse_iso(value: str | None) -> dt.date | None:
    return None if value is None else dt.date.fromisoformat(value)


@dataclass
class Quote:
    date: dt.date
    close: Decimal | None
    change: Decimal | None
    change_pct: Decimal | None
    volume: int | None

    def to_json(self) -> dict[str, Any]:
        return {
            "date": self.date.isoformat(),
            "close": num(self.close),
            "change": num(self.change),
            "change_pct": num(self.change_pct),
            "volume": self.volume,
        }

    @classmethod
    def from_json(cls, d: dict[str, Any]) -> Quote:
        return cls(
            date=dt.date.fromisoformat(d["date"]),
            close=dec(d.get("close")),
            change=dec(d.get("change")),
            change_pct=dec(d.get("change_pct")),
            volume=d.get("volume"),
        )


@dataclass
class Company:
    instrument_code: str
    symbol: str
    name: str
    market: str
    currency: str | None = None
    sector: str | None = None
    type: str | None = None
    website: str | None = None
    listed: bool = True
    listed_since: dt.date | None = None
    delisted_on: dt.date | None = None
    quote: Quote | None = None

    def to_json(self) -> dict[str, Any]:
        return {
            "instrument_code": self.instrument_code,
            "symbol": self.symbol,
            "name": self.name,
            "market": self.market,
            "currency": self.currency,
            "sector": self.sector,
            "type": self.type,
            "website": self.website,
            "listed": self.listed,
            "listed_since": isodate(self.listed_since),
            "delisted_on": isodate(self.delisted_on),
            "quote": self.quote.to_json() if self.quote else None,
        }

    @classmethod
    def from_json(cls, d: dict[str, Any]) -> Company:
        quote = d.get("quote")
        return cls(
            instrument_code=d["instrument_code"],
            symbol=d["symbol"],
            name=d["name"],
            market=d["market"],
            currency=d.get("currency"),
            sector=d.get("sector"),
            type=d.get("type"),
            website=d.get("website"),
            listed=bool(d.get("listed", True)),
            listed_since=parse_iso(d.get("listed_since")),
            delisted_on=parse_iso(d.get("delisted_on")),
            quote=Quote.from_json(quote) if quote else None,
        )


@dataclass
class Index:
    name: str
    date: dt.date
    value: Decimal | None
    change: Decimal | None
    change_pct: Decimal | None
    volume: int | None

    def to_json(self) -> dict[str, Any]:
        return {
            "name": self.name,
            "date": self.date.isoformat(),
            "value": num(self.value),
            "change": num(self.change),
            "change_pct": num(self.change_pct),
            "volume": self.volume,
        }

    @classmethod
    def from_json(cls, d: dict[str, Any]) -> Index:
        return cls(
            name=d["name"],
            date=dt.date.fromisoformat(d["date"]),
            value=dec(d.get("value")),
            change=dec(d.get("change")),
            change_pct=dec(d.get("change_pct")),
            volume=d.get("volume"),
        )


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class CorporateAction:
    symbol: str
    type: str
    amount: Decimal | None
    currency: str | None
    record_date: dt.date | None
    ex_date: dt.date | None
    payment_date: dt.date | None
    instrument_code: str | None = None

    def key(self) -> tuple:
        """Identity for de-duplication. Excludes instrument_code, which is derived."""
        return (self.symbol, self.type, self.record_date, self.ex_date, self.payment_date, self.amount, self.currency)

    def to_json(self) -> dict[str, Any]:
        return {
            "instrument_code": self.instrument_code,
            "symbol": self.symbol,
            "type": self.type,
            "amount": num(self.amount),
            "currency": self.currency,
            "record_date": isodate(self.record_date),
            "ex_date": isodate(self.ex_date),
            "payment_date": isodate(self.payment_date),
        }

    @classmethod
    def from_json(cls, d: dict[str, Any]) -> CorporateAction:
        return cls(
            symbol=d["symbol"],
            type=d["type"],
            amount=dec(d.get("amount")),
            currency=d.get("currency"),
            record_date=parse_iso(d.get("record_date")),
            ex_date=parse_iso(d.get("ex_date")),
            payment_date=parse_iso(d.get("payment_date")),
            instrument_code=d.get("instrument_code"),
        )
```

### Task 4: Honest fetcher

**Why:** the only code that talks to the JSE site. It sends one honest User-Agent, a timeout and a random pause between requests, and retries only transient failures. A block is never retried or worked around, and after one the `Fetcher` refuses every later request (spec §3.2, §6.3). Error messages name the request path, never the host, because `/v2/status` shows them. The tests use a fake opener and a server on 127.0.0.1; nothing reaches the network.

**Files:**
- Create: `jamstockex/fetch.py`
- Test: `tests/test_fetch.py`, `tests/fixtures/cloudfront-blocked.html`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `jamstockex.config.SourceConfig` (Task 2); `tests.helpers.fixture_html` (Task 2).
- Produces:
  - `class FetchError(Exception)`; `class BlockedError(FetchError)`
  - `BLOCK_STATUSES = frozenset({403, 429})`; `is_block_page(text: str) -> bool` (CloudFront "Request blocked" page)
  - `class Fetcher(source: SourceConfig, opener: Callable[..., Any] | None = None, sleep: Callable[[float], None] = time.sleep)` with `get(path: str) -> str`, and attributes `request_count: int` and `blocked: bool`. `opener(request, timeout=…)` defaults to `urllib.request.build_opener().open`.

`tests/test_fetch.py`:

```python
import http.client
import io
import socket
import threading
import urllib.error
from email.message import Message
from http.server import BaseHTTPRequestHandler, HTTPServer

import pytest

from jamstockex.config import SourceConfig
from jamstockex.fetch import BlockedError, FetchError, Fetcher, is_block_page
from tests.helpers import fixture_html

SOURCE = SourceConfig(
    base_url="https://www.example.test",
    user_agent="test-agent/1.0",
    timeout_s=7,
    retries=3,
    delay_s=(2.0, 2.0),
)


class FakeResponse:
    def __init__(self, status, body="", charset="utf-8"):
        self.status = status
        self._body = body.encode(charset)
        self.headers = Message()
        self.headers["Content-Type"] = f"text/html; charset={charset}"

    def read(self):
        return self._body

    def __enter__(self):
        return self

    def __exit__(self, *exc):
        return False


def http_error(status, body=""):
    return urllib.error.HTTPError("https://www.example.test/x", status, "error", Message(), io.BytesIO(body.encode()))


class FakeOpener:
    """Stands in for urllib's opener: returns or raises the queued outcomes in order."""

    def __init__(self, outcomes):
        self.outcomes = list(outcomes)
        self.calls = []

    def __call__(self, request, timeout=None):
        self.calls.append({"url": request.full_url, "user_agent": request.get_header("User-agent"),
                           "timeout": timeout})
        outcome = self.outcomes.pop(0)
        if isinstance(outcome, BaseException):
            raise outcome
        return outcome


def make(outcomes, source=SOURCE):
    opener = FakeOpener(outcomes)
    sleeps = []
    return Fetcher(source, opener=opener, sleep=sleeps.append), opener, sleeps


def test_returns_body_and_sends_user_agent_and_timeout():
    fetcher, opener, sleeps = make([FakeResponse(200, "<html>ok</html>")])
    assert fetcher.get("/path/?x=1") == "<html>ok</html>"
    assert opener.calls == [{"url": "https://www.example.test/path/?x=1", "user_agent": "test-agent/1.0",
                             "timeout": 7}]
    assert sleeps == []
    assert fetcher.request_count == 1


def test_decodes_with_the_declared_charset():
    fetcher, _, _ = make([FakeResponse(200, "café", charset="iso-8859-1")])
    assert fetcher.get("/a") == "café"


def test_delays_between_requests_but_not_before_the_first():
    fetcher, _, sleeps = make([FakeResponse(200, "a"), FakeResponse(200, "b")])
    fetcher.get("/a")
    fetcher.get("/b")
    assert sleeps == [2.0]
    assert fetcher.request_count == 2


def test_retries_5xx_with_backoff_then_succeeds():
    fetcher, _, sleeps = make([http_error(502), FakeResponse(200, "ok")])
    assert fetcher.get("/a") == "ok"
    assert fetcher.request_count == 2
    assert sleeps == [2.0, 2]  # politeness delay, then backoff 2**1


@pytest.mark.parametrize("error", [
    urllib.error.URLError(ConnectionRefusedError("refused")),
    TimeoutError("timed out"),
    http.client.RemoteDisconnected("closed"),
    ConnectionResetError("reset"),
])
def test_retries_connection_errors_and_timeouts(error):
    fetcher, _, _ = make([error, FakeResponse(200, "ok")])
    assert fetcher.get("/a") == "ok"


def test_gives_up_after_configured_attempts():
    fetcher, _, _ = make([http_error(500), http_error(503), http_error(500)])
    with pytest.raises(FetchError, match="after 3 attempts: HTTP 500"):
        fetcher.get("/a")
    assert fetcher.request_count == 3


def test_404_is_a_fetch_error_without_retry():
    fetcher, opener, _ = make([http_error(404)])
    with pytest.raises(FetchError, match="HTTP 404"):
        fetcher.get("/missing")
    assert len(opener.calls) == 1


@pytest.mark.parametrize("status", [403, 429])
def test_block_status_raises_immediately_and_refuses_further_requests(status):
    fetcher, opener, _ = make([http_error(status)])
    with pytest.raises(BlockedError):
        fetcher.get("/a")
    assert fetcher.blocked is True
    with pytest.raises(BlockedError, match="earlier request was blocked"):
        fetcher.get("/b")
    assert len(opener.calls) == 1
    assert fetcher.request_count == 1


def test_cloudfront_block_page_is_blocked_even_with_200():
    fetcher, _, _ = make([FakeResponse(200, fixture_html("cloudfront-blocked.html"))])
    with pytest.raises(BlockedError):
        fetcher.get("/a")


def test_is_block_page():
    assert is_block_page(fixture_html("cloudfront-blocked.html"))
    assert not is_block_page("<html><table></table></html>")


def test_blocked_error_is_a_fetch_error():
    assert issubclass(BlockedError, FetchError)


JSE = SourceConfig(base_url="https://www.jamstockex.com", user_agent="test-agent/1.0", timeout_s=7,
                   retries=2, delay_s=(0.0, 0.0))
HOST_IN_MESSAGE = socket.gaierror(8, "nodename nor servname provided for www.jamstockex.com")


@pytest.mark.parametrize("outcomes", [
    [http_error(500), http_error(503)],
    [http_error(403)],
    [http_error(404)],
    [urllib.error.URLError(HOST_IN_MESSAGE), urllib.error.URLError(HOST_IN_MESSAGE)],
], ids=["5xx-give-up", "403", "404", "dns-error"])
def test_error_messages_never_contain_the_site_host(outcomes):
    """Messages are shown in /v2/status, which must not link to the JSE site."""
    fetcher, _, _ = make(outcomes, source=JSE)
    with pytest.raises(FetchError) as caught:
        fetcher.get("/trading/trade-quotes/?market=50")
    assert "jamstockex.com" not in str(caught.value)
    assert "/trading/trade-quotes/?market=50" in str(caught.value)


# --- the real urllib path, against a local server on 127.0.0.1 -----------------


class _Handler(BaseHTTPRequestHandler):
    seen: list = []

    def do_GET(self):
        _Handler.seen.append((self.path, self.headers.get("User-Agent")))
        if self.path == "/ok":
            body = "<html>ok é</html>".encode("utf-8")
            self.send_response(200)
            self.send_header("Content-Type", "text/html; charset=utf-8")
        elif self.path == "/blocked":
            body = b"Forbidden"
            self.send_response(403)
        else:
            body = b"busy"
            self.send_response(503)
        self.send_header("Content-Length", str(len(body)))
        self.end_headers()
        self.wfile.write(body)

    def log_message(self, *args):
        pass


@pytest.fixture
def local_server():
    _Handler.seen = []
    server = HTTPServer(("127.0.0.1", 0), _Handler)
    thread = threading.Thread(target=server.serve_forever, kwargs={"poll_interval": 0.05}, daemon=True)
    thread.start()
    yield f"http://127.0.0.1:{server.server_address[1]}"
    server.shutdown()
    server.server_close()


def test_real_urllib_requests_against_a_local_server(local_server):
    source = SourceConfig(base_url=local_server, user_agent="test-agent/1.0", timeout_s=5, retries=2,
                          delay_s=(0.0, 0.0))
    fetcher = Fetcher(source, sleep=lambda seconds: None)
    assert fetcher.get("/ok") == "<html>ok é</html>"
    with pytest.raises(FetchError, match="after 2 attempts: HTTP 503"):
        fetcher.get("/busy")
    with pytest.raises(BlockedError):
        fetcher.get("/blocked")
    assert _Handler.seen == [("/ok", "test-agent/1.0"), ("/busy", "test-agent/1.0"),
                             ("/busy", "test-agent/1.0"), ("/blocked", "test-agent/1.0")]
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the fetcher**

`jamstockex/fetch.py`:

```python
"""Honest HTTP client for the JSE website, on urllib.request.

One honest User-Agent, a timeout, a random pause between requests, and retries
only for transient failures. A block (403/429 or a CloudFront "Request blocked"
page) is never retried or worked around, and after one this Fetcher refuses to
send any more requests (JSE Terms & Conditions §8.2).

Error messages name the request path, never the host or URL: they are shown in
/v2/status, which must not link to the JSE site.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import codecs
import http.client
import logging
import random
import time
import urllib.error
import urllib.request
from typing import Any, Callable

from jamstockex.config import SourceConfig

log = logging.getLogger(__name__)

BLOCK_STATUSES = frozenset({403, 429})


class FetchError(Exception):
    """A page could not be fetched (after retries, or a status that isn't retried)."""


class BlockedError(FetchError):
    """The site is blocking this client. Never retried or worked around."""


def is_block_page(text: str) -> bool:
    return "Request blocked" in text and "cloudfront" in text.lower()


def _decode(body: bytes, charset: str | None) -> str:
    try:
        codecs.lookup(charset or "utf-8")
    except LookupError:
        charset = "utf-8"
    return body.decode(charset or "utf-8", errors="replace")


def _problem(exc: BaseException) -> str:
    """Exception class names only: exception text can embed the host name."""
    reason = getattr(exc, "reason", None)
    if isinstance(exc, urllib.error.URLError) and isinstance(reason, BaseException):
        return f"{type(exc).__name__} ({type(reason).__name__})"
    return type(exc).__name__


class Fetcher:
    """`opener(request, timeout=...)` returns a response; the default is urllib's own opener."""

    def __init__(
        self,
        source: SourceConfig,
        opener: Callable[..., Any] | None = None,
        sleep: Callable[[float], None] = time.sleep,
    ) -> None:
        self.source = source
        self._open = opener if opener is not None else urllib.request.build_opener().open
        self.sleep = sleep
        self.request_count = 0
        self.blocked = False

    def get(self, path: str) -> str:
        if self.blocked:
            raise BlockedError(f"Not requesting {path}: an earlier request was blocked")
        url = self.source.base_url + "/" + path.lstrip("/")
        problem = "no attempts made"
        for attempt in range(self.source.retries):
            if self.request_count:
                self.sleep(random.uniform(*self.source.delay_s))
            if attempt:
                self.sleep(2**attempt)
            self.request_count += 1
            log.info("GET %s", path)
            request = urllib.request.Request(url, headers={"User-Agent": self.source.user_agent})
            try:
                with self._open(request, timeout=self.source.timeout_s) as response:
                    status = response.status
                    text = _decode(response.read(), response.headers.get_content_charset())
            except urllib.error.HTTPError as exc:
                status = exc.code
                try:
                    text = _decode(exc.read(), None)
                except (OSError, http.client.HTTPException):
                    text = ""
            except (urllib.error.URLError, http.client.HTTPException, OSError) as exc:
                problem = _problem(exc)
                log.warning("Request for %s failed (attempt %d/%d): %s", path, attempt + 1,
                            self.source.retries, problem)
                continue
            if status in BLOCK_STATUSES or is_block_page(text):
                self.blocked = True
                raise BlockedError(f"HTTP {status} for {path}: the site is blocking automated requests")
            if status >= 500:
                problem = f"HTTP {status}"
                log.warning("Server error for %s (attempt %d/%d): %s", path, attempt + 1,
                            self.source.retries, problem)
                continue
            if status != 200:
                raise FetchError(f"HTTP {status} for {path}")
            return text
        raise FetchError(f"Gave up on {path} after {self.source.retries} attempts: {problem}")
```

### Task 5: HTML table extractor

**Why:** replaces BeautifulSoup with the standard library's `html.parser` for the one thing the parsers need: every `<table>` in document order, as rows of cells with whitespace-collapsed text and the link targets in each cell (spec §3.2).

**Files:**
- Create: `jamstockex/html.py`
- Test: `tests/test_html.py`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: nothing from earlier tasks.
- Produces:
  - `@dataclass(frozen=True) Cell(text: str, hrefs: tuple[str, ...] = (), header: bool = False)` (`header` is true for `<th>`)
  - `@dataclass Row(cells: list[Cell])`; `@dataclass Table(rows: list[Row])`
  - `extract_tables(html: str) -> list[Table]`: rows from `<thead>`/`<tbody>`/`<tfoot>` in document order; unclosed `<td>`/`<th>`/`<tr>` tolerated; a nested table is its own `Table` (after its parent) and its text is not added to the parent cell; `<script>`/`<style>` text ignored

from jamstockex.html import Cell, Row, Table, extract_tables


def texts(table):
    return [[cell.text for cell in row.cells] for row in table.rows]


def test_simple_table_with_header_cells():
    [table] = extract_tables("<table><tr><th>Symbol</th><th>Close</th></tr><tr><td>ACME</td><td>1.5</td></tr></table>")
    assert table == Table(rows=[
        Row(cells=[Cell(text="Symbol", header=True), Cell(text="Close", header=True)]),
        Row(cells=[Cell(text="ACME"), Cell(text="1.5")]),
    ])


def test_thead_tbody_tfoot_rows_come_in_document_order():
    html = """<table>
      <thead><tr><th>H</th></tr></thead>
      <tfoot><tr><td>F</td></tr></tfoot>
      <tbody><tr><td>B1</td></tr><tr><td>B2</td></tr></tbody>
    </table>"""
    [table] = extract_tables(html)
    assert texts(table) == [["H"], ["F"], ["B1"], ["B2"]]


def test_unclosed_cells_and_rows_are_closed_by_what_follows():
    [table] = extract_tables("<table><tr><td>a<td>b<tr><td>c</table><p>after</p>")
    assert texts(table) == [["a", "b"], ["c"]]


def test_unclosed_table_at_end_of_input_is_kept():
    [table] = extract_tables("<table><tr><th>X<tr><td>1")
    assert texts(table) == [["X"], ["1"]]


def test_tbody_closes_an_open_row():
    [table] = extract_tables("<table><thead><tr><th>A</thead><tbody><tr><td>1</tbody></table>")
    assert texts(table) == [["A"], ["1"]]


def test_nested_tables_are_separate_and_not_part_of_the_parent_cell():
    html = """<table>
      <tr><td>outer <table><tr><td>inner 1</td></tr><tr><td>inner 2</td></tr></table> cell</td><td>next</td></tr>
      <tr><td>outer row 2</td></tr>
    </table>"""
    outer, inner = extract_tables(html)
    assert texts(outer) == [["outer cell", "next"], ["outer row 2"]]
    assert texts(inner) == [["inner 1"], ["inner 2"]]


def test_hrefs_are_collected_per_cell():
    html = """<table><tr>
      <td><a href="/i/?instrument=9001">ACME</a> <a href=" https://acme.example ">site</a></td>
      <td>no link</td><td><a>no href</a><a href="">empty</a></td>
    </tr></table>"""
    [table] = extract_tables(html)
    cells = table.rows[0].cells
    assert cells[0].hrefs == ("/i/?instrument=9001", "https://acme.example")
    assert cells[0].text == "ACME site"
    assert cells[1].hrefs == ()
    assert cells[2].hrefs == ()


def test_whitespace_and_entities_are_collapsed():
    [table] = extract_tables("<table><tr><td>\n  Acme\n\t Holdings&nbsp;Ltd<br>Group &amp; Co  </td></tr></table>")
    assert table.rows[0].cells[0].text == "Acme Holdings Ltd Group & Co"


def test_script_and_style_text_is_ignored():
    html = "<table><tr><td>a<script>var x = '<td>no</td>';</script><style>td{}</style>b</td></tr></table>"
    [table] = extract_tables(html)
    assert texts(table) == [["a b"]]


def test_page_without_tables():
    assert extract_tables("<html><body><p>Down for maintenance</p></body></html>") == []

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the extractor**

`jamstockex/html.py`:

```python
"""Minimal HTML table extractor on the standard library's html.parser.

It replaces BeautifulSoup for the one thing the parsers need: every <table> in
document order, as rows of cells with whitespace-collapsed text and the link
targets in each cell.

- Rows from <thead>, <tbody> and <tfoot> come in document order.
- Unclosed <td>, <th> and <tr> are closed by the next cell, row or table end.
- A nested table is extracted as its own Table (after its parent, in start-tag
  order), and its text is not added to the parent cell.
- Text inside <script> and <style> is ignored.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from html.parser import HTMLParser


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Cell:
    text: str
    hrefs: tuple[str, ...] = ()
    header: bool = False  # a <th> cell


@dataclass
class Row:
    cells: list[Cell] = field(default_factory=list)


@dataclass
class Table:
    rows: list[Row] = field(default_factory=list)


def extract_tables(html: str) -> list[Table]:
    parser = _TableParser()
    parser.feed(html)
    parser.close()
    return parser.tables


class _OpenTable:
    def __init__(self, table: Table) -> None:
        self.table = table
        self.row: list[Cell] | None = None
        self.parts: list[str] | None = None  # text of the open cell
        self.hrefs: list[str] = []
        self.header = False

    def close_cell(self) -> None:
        if self.parts is None:
            return
        text = " ".join(" ".join(self.parts).split())
        if self.row is None:
            self.row = []
        self.row.append(Cell(text=text, hrefs=tuple(self.hrefs), header=self.header))
        self.parts, self.hrefs, self.header = None, [], False

    def close_row(self) -> None:
        self.close_cell()
        if self.row is not None:
            self.table.rows.append(Row(cells=self.row))
            self.row = None

    def open_cell(self, header: bool) -> None:
        self.close_cell()
        if self.row is None:
            self.row = []
        self.parts, self.hrefs, self.header = [], [], header


class _TableParser(HTMLParser):
    def __init__(self) -> None:
        super().__init__(convert_charrefs=True)
        self.tables: list[Table] = []
        self.stack: list[_OpenTable] = []
        self.skip_depth = 0  # inside <script> or <style>

    def handle_starttag(self, tag: str, attrs: list[tuple[str, str | None]]) -> None:
        if tag in ("script", "style"):
            self.skip_depth += 1
            return
        if tag == "table":
            table = Table()
            self.tables.append(table)
            self.stack.append(_OpenTable(table))
            return
        if not self.stack:
            return
        current = self.stack[-1]
        if tag == "tr":
            current.close_row()
            current.row = []
        elif tag in ("td", "th"):
            current.open_cell(header=tag == "th")
        elif tag in ("thead", "tbody", "tfoot"):
            current.close_row()
        elif tag == "a" and current.parts is not None:
            href = dict(attrs).get("href")
            if href and href.strip():
                current.hrefs.append(href.strip())
        elif tag == "br" and current.parts is not None:
            current.parts.append(" ")

    def handle_endtag(self, tag: str) -> None:
        if tag in ("script", "style"):
            self.skip_depth = max(0, self.skip_depth - 1)
            return
        if not self.stack:
            return
        current = self.stack[-1]
        if tag == "table":
            current.close_row()
            self.stack.pop()
        elif tag in ("td", "th"):
            current.close_cell()
        elif tag in ("tr", "thead", "tbody", "tfoot"):
            current.close_row()

    def handle_data(self, data: str) -> None:
        if self.skip_depth or not self.stack:
            return
        current = self.stack[-1]
        if current.parts is not None:
            current.parts.append(data)

    def close(self) -> None:
        super().close()
        while self.stack:
            self.stack.pop().close_row()
```

### Task 6: Parser: table helpers and value parsers

**Why:** the page parsers (Tasks 7–9) find columns by **normalised header name** through alias tuples, not by position, so an inserted or reordered column doesn't break them. Value parsers return `None` for anything they can't parse, never `0` (superseded spec §3.2). This task creates `jamstockex/parse.py` with the shared helpers; later tasks append the page parsers.

**Files:**
- Create: `jamstockex/parse.py` (helpers and value parsers; the module's imports already cover Tasks 7–9)
- Test: `tests/test_parse_values.py`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `jamstockex.html.Cell`, `Row`, `Table`, `extract_tables` (Task 5); `jamstockex.models.Company`, `CorporateAction`, `Index`, `Quote` (Task 3).
- Produces:
  - Logger `jamstockex.parse` (Task 13 collects its warnings into the refresh report)
  - `class ParseError(Exception)`: `ParseError(page: str, problem: str)`; attributes `page`, `problem`; `str()` is `"<page>: <problem>"`
  - Type aliases `Columns = dict[str, int | None]`, `Aliases = dict[str, tuple[str, ...]]`
  - `normalize_header(text: str) -> str` (lowercase, `%` → `pct`, only `[a-z0-9]` kept); `header_index(table: Table) -> dict[str, int]` (from the first row)
  - `resolve_columns(header: dict[str, int], aliases: Aliases) -> Columns`; `matching_tables(tables: list[Table], aliases: Aliases, required: tuple[str, ...]) -> list[tuple[Table, Columns]]`
  - `data_rows(table: Table) -> list[Row]` (rows after the header that have `<td>` cells and some text)
  - `warn_missing_columns(page: str, column_sets: list[Columns], ignore: tuple[str, ...] = ()) -> None` (one warning: `"<page>: column(s) not found, values will be null: <fields>"`)
  - `cell_text(cells: list[Cell], idx: int | None) -> str`; `cell_href(cells: list[Cell], idx: int | None) -> str | None`
  - `instrument_code_from_href(href: str | None) -> str | None` (the `instrument` query parameter); `row_instrument_code(row: Row) -> str | None` (first such link in the row)
  - `to_decimal(text: str | None) -> Decimal | None`; `to_int(text: str | None) -> int | None`; `parse_date(text: str | None) -> date | None` (`%a %d %b %Y`, `%d %b %Y`, `%Y-%m-%d`, `%A %d %B %Y`, `%d %B %Y`); `parse_amount(text: str | None) -> tuple[str | None, Decimal | None]` (`"JMD 0.7500"` → `("JMD", Decimal("0.7500"))`); `normalize_action_type(text: str | None) -> str` (lowercase snake_case)

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the helpers and value parsers**

`jamstockex/parse.py`:

```python
"""Parsing of JSE website pages.

Columns are located by normalised header text, not position, so an inserted
or reordered column doesn't break parsing. When the site renames a header,
add the new normalised name to the relevant alias tuple. Value parsers
return None for anything they can't parse; they never invent a 0.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import datetime as dt
import logging
import re
from decimal import Decimal, InvalidOperation
from urllib.parse import parse_qs, urlparse

from jamstockex.html import Cell, Row, Table, extract_tables
from jamstockex.models import Company, CorporateAction, Index, Quote

log = logging.getLogger(__name__)

Columns = dict[str, int | None]
Aliases = dict[str, tuple[str, ...]]

_EMPTY = {"", "-", "--", "n/a", "na", "nil"}
_DATE_FORMATS = ("%a %d %b %Y", "%d %b %Y", "%Y-%m-%d", "%A %d %B %Y", "%d %B %Y")


class ParseError(Exception):
    """A page doesn't have the structure we expect (usually a site layout change)."""

    def __init__(self, page: str, problem: str) -> None:
        super().__init__(f"{page}: {problem}")
        self.page = page
        self.problem = problem


# --- table helpers -------------------------------------------------------


def normalize_header(text: str) -> str:
    return re.sub(r"[^a-z0-9]", "", text.lower().replace("%", "pct"))


def header_index(table: Table) -> dict[str, int]:
    """Normalised header text -> column position, from the table's first row."""
    if not table.rows:
        return {}
    index: dict[str, int] = {}
    for position, cell in enumerate(table.rows[0].cells):
        index.setdefault(normalize_header(cell.text), position)
    return index


def resolve_columns(header: dict[str, int], aliases: Aliases) -> Columns:
    return {field: next((header[name] for name in names if name in header), None) for field, names in aliases.items()}


def matching_tables(tables: list[Table], aliases: Aliases, required: tuple[str, ...]) -> list[tuple[Table, Columns]]:
    matches = []
    for table in tables:
        columns = resolve_columns(header_index(table), aliases)
        if all(columns[field] is not None for field in required):
            matches.append((table, columns))
    return matches


def data_rows(table: Table) -> list[Row]:
    """Rows after the header that have <td> cells and some text (blank spacer rows are dropped)."""
    return [
        row for row in table.rows[1:]
        if any(not cell.header for cell in row.cells) and any(cell.text for cell in row.cells)
    ]


def warn_missing_columns(page: str, column_sets: list[Columns], ignore: tuple[str, ...] = ()) -> None:
    """Warn once per page about optional fields whose header matched no alias.

    Required fields raise ParseError instead. Without this warning, a renamed optional
    header would silently turn every value in that column into null.
    """
    missing = sorted({f for cols in column_sets for f, idx in cols.items() if idx is None} - set(ignore))
    if missing:
        log.warning("%s: column(s) not found, values will be null: %s", page, ", ".join(missing))


def cell_text(cells: list[Cell], idx: int | None) -> str:
    if idx is None or idx >= len(cells):
        return ""
    return cells[idx].text


def cell_href(cells: list[Cell], idx: int | None) -> str | None:
    if idx is None or idx >= len(cells) or not cells[idx].hrefs:
        return None
    return cells[idx].hrefs[0]


def instrument_code_from_href(href: str | None) -> str | None:
    if not href:
        return None
    values = parse_qs(urlparse(href).query).get("instrument")
    if not values:
        return None
    return values[0].strip() or None


def row_instrument_code(row: Row) -> str | None:
    for cell in row.cells:
        for href in cell.hrefs:
            code = instrument_code_from_href(href)
            if code:
                return code
    return None


# --- value parsers -------------------------------------------------------


def to_decimal(text: str | None) -> Decimal | None:
    if text is None:
        return None
    cleaned = text.strip().replace(",", "").replace("$", "").replace("%", "").replace(" ", "")
    negative = cleaned.startswith("(") and cleaned.endswith(")")
    if negative:
        cleaned = cleaned[1:-1]
    if cleaned.lower() in _EMPTY:
        return None
    try:
        value = Decimal(cleaned)
    except InvalidOperation:
        return None
    if not value.is_finite():
        return None
    return -value if negative else value


def to_int(text: str | None) -> int | None:
    value = to_decimal(text)
    if value is None or value != value.to_integral_value():
        return None
    return int(value)


def parse_date(text: str | None) -> dt.date | None:
    cleaned = " ".join((text or "").split())
    for fmt in _DATE_FORMATS:
        try:
            return dt.datetime.strptime(cleaned, fmt).date()
        except ValueError:
            continue
    return None


def parse_amount(text: str | None) -> tuple[str | None, Decimal | None]:
    """'JMD 0.7500' -> ('JMD', Decimal('0.7500')); '0.12' -> (None, Decimal('0.12'))."""
    parts = (text or "").split()
    if not parts:
        return None, None
    if len(parts) >= 2 and parts[0].isalpha():
        return parts[0].upper(), to_decimal(parts[-1])
    return None, to_decimal(parts[-1])


def normalize_action_type(text: str | None) -> str:
    return re.sub(r"[^a-z0-9]+", "_", (text or "").lower()).strip("_")
```

### Task 7: Parser: listed companies

**Why:** the listings page gives each instrument's code (a numeric ID from the `?instrument=` link in the Security cell; the fixtures use `9001` and `9002`), symbol, name, currency, sector, type and website. The site sends every table twice (a desktop and a mobile copy), so rows are de-duplicated by `instrument_code` (the first table wins) and a skipped row that appears in both copies is counted once. A link to the JSE site is never kept as a company website (spec §5.2). A page with no matching table raises `ParseError`; a header-only table is an empty market, not an error. The fixtures copy the real layout checked in Task 1 (headers `Security`, `Symbol`, `ISIN`, `Currency`, `Sector`, `Type`, `Website`, both copies of the table), with fake companies.

**Files:**
- Modify: `jamstockex/parse.py` (append)
- Test: `tests/test_parse_listings.py`, `tests/fixtures/listed-companies.html`, `tests/fixtures/listed-companies-empty.html`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: from `jamstockex/parse.py` (Task 6): `ParseError`, `Aliases`, `matching_tables`, `warn_missing_columns`, `data_rows`, `cell_text`, `cell_href`, `row_instrument_code`, `extract_tables`, `Company`.
- Produces:
  - `LISTING_COLUMNS: Aliases` (fields `name`, `symbol`, `currency`, `sector`, `type`, `website`; `name` and `symbol` required)
  - `parse_listed_companies(html: str, market_label: str) -> list[Company]` (each `Company.market == market_label`, `listed=True`, no quote). Warns once per page about unmatched optional columns and about skipped rows (no instrument link or no symbol; a row repeated in the mobile copy counts once).

- [ ] **Step 3: Append the listings parser**

Append to the end of `jamstockex/parse.py`:

```python


# --- listed companies ----------------------------------------------------

LISTING_COLUMNS: Aliases = {
    "name": ("security", "instrument", "instrumentname", "company", "name"),
    "symbol": ("symbol", "ticker"),
    "currency": ("currency",),
    "sector": ("sector",),
    "type": ("type", "instrumenttype", "securitytype"),
    "website": ("website", "url"),
}


def _website(cells: list[Cell], idx: int | None) -> str | None:
    href = cell_href(cells, idx)
    if not href or not href.startswith(("http://", "https://")):
        return None
    if "jamstockex.com" in urlparse(href).netloc.lower():
        return None
    return href


def parse_listed_companies(html: str, market_label: str) -> list[Company]:
    page = extract_tables(html)
    tables = matching_tables(page, LISTING_COLUMNS, ("name", "symbol"))
    if not tables:
        raise ParseError("listed-companies", "no table with 'security' and 'symbol' columns")
    warn_missing_columns(f"listed-companies ({market_label})", [cols for _, cols in tables])
    companies: dict[str, Company] = {}
    skipped: set[tuple[str, ...]] = set()  # a row repeated in the mobile copy counts once
    for table, cols in tables:
        for row in data_rows(table):
            cells = row.cells
            code = row_instrument_code(row)
            symbol = cell_text(cells, cols["symbol"])
            if not code or not symbol:
                skipped.add(tuple(cell.text for cell in cells))
                continue
            companies.setdefault(code, Company(
                instrument_code=code,
                symbol=symbol,
                name=cell_text(cells, cols["name"]),
                market=market_label,
                currency=cell_text(cells, cols["currency"]) or None,
                sector=cell_text(cells, cols["sector"]) or None,
                type=cell_text(cells, cols["type"]) or None,
                website=_website(cells, cols["website"]),
            ))
    if skipped:
        log.warning("listed-companies (%s): skipped %d row(s) without an instrument link or symbol",
                    market_label, len(skipped))
    return list(companies.values())
```

### Task 8: Parser: trade quotes and indices

**Why:** the combined-market trade-quotes page holds the day's quotes in one table per section (ordinary, US-dollar, preference shares), plus an indices table; the site sends every table twice (desktop and mobile copies). Each quotes row carries its `?instrument=` link on the Symbol cell; the first header cell is empty. On a date with no trading (a holiday) the site sends **no tables at all**, only its message "Looks like there is no data coming back." (Task 1). So the **quotes table is required**: a page without one returns `({}, [])` (logged at INFO) only when it has that message, and otherwise raises `ParseError`, even if the indices parse. That way a renamed price header or a new error page can't pass for a holiday; Task 11 adds a safety net in case the message starts appearing every day. A missing indices table only warns. `change_pct` comes from the page when it has a `%` column, otherwise it is computed as `change / (close − change) × 100` (superseded spec §3.2, §5). The fixtures copy the real layout and header text (multi-line `<th>` cells with `<br />`, `Change (%)` on the indices), with fake companies.

**Files:**
- Modify: `jamstockex/parse.py` (append)
- Test: `tests/test_parse_quotes.py`, `tests/fixtures/trade-quotes.html`, `tests/fixtures/trade-quotes-holiday.html`, `tests/fixtures/trade-quotes-extra-column.html`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: from `jamstockex/parse.py` (Task 6): `ParseError`, `Aliases`, `matching_tables`, `header_index`, `warn_missing_columns`, `data_rows`, `cell_text`, `row_instrument_code`, `to_decimal`, `to_int`, `extract_tables`, `Quote`, `Index`.
- Produces:

  - `compute_change_pct(close: Decimal | None, change: Decimal | None) -> Decimal | None` (2 decimal places; `None` when the previous price is 0 or a value is missing)
  - `NO_DATA_MARKER: str` (`"looks like there is no data coming back"`) and `is_no_data_page(html: str) -> bool` (marker found in the lower-cased, whitespace-squashed page)
  - `parse_trade_quotes(html: str, trade_date: date) -> tuple[dict[str, Quote], list[Index]]`: quotes keyed by `instrument_code`, every `Quote.date` and `Index.date` is `trade_date`; a page with no quotes table returns `({}, [])` if `is_no_data_page`, otherwise raises `ParseError`
