`tests/test_parse_quotes.py`:

```python
import datetime as dt
from decimal import Decimal

import pytest

from jamstockex.models import Index, Quote
from jamstockex.parse import ParseError, compute_change_pct, is_no_data_page, parse_trade_quotes
from tests.helpers import fixture_html

DAY = dt.date(2026, 11, 9)


def test_parses_quotes_across_tables_keyed_by_instrument_code():
    quotes, _ = parse_trade_quotes(fixture_html("trade-quotes.html"), DAY)
    assert set(quotes) == {"9001", "9002"}  # row without link skipped
    assert quotes["9001"] == Quote(date=DAY, close=Decimal("12.50"), change=Decimal("-0.25"),
                                       change_pct=Decimal("-1.96"), volume=48213)
    assert quotes["9002"] == Quote(date=DAY, close=Decimal("0.0850"), change=Decimal("0.0050"),
                                       change_pct=Decimal("6.25"), volume=1000)


def test_parses_indices():
    _, indices = parse_trade_quotes(fixture_html("trade-quotes.html"), DAY)
    assert indices == [
        Index(name="JSE Main Index", date=DAY, value=Decimal("325456.78"), change=Decimal("-1234.56"),
              change_pct=Decimal("-0.38"), volume=12345678),
        Index(name="JSE Junior Market Index", date=DAY, value=Decimal("3901.12"), change=Decimal("10.50"),
              change_pct=Decimal("0.27"), volume=2000000),
    ]


def test_inserted_and_reordered_columns_still_parse_and_page_pct_is_used(caplog):
    quotes, indices = parse_trade_quotes(fixture_html("trade-quotes-extra-column.html"), DAY)
    assert indices == []
    assert "trade-quotes: no indices table (with 'index' and 'value' columns) found" in caplog.messages
    assert quotes["9001"] == Quote(date=DAY, close=Decimal("12.50"), change=Decimal("-0.25"),
                                       change_pct=Decimal("-1.95"), volume=48213)


def test_no_data_page_is_a_day_without_trading(caplog):
    with caplog.at_level("INFO", logger="jamstockex.parse"):
        assert parse_trade_quotes(fixture_html("trade-quotes-holiday.html"), DAY) == ({}, [])
    assert caplog.messages == ["trade-quotes: the site reports no data for 2026-11-09 (no trading that day)"]


def test_page_without_tables_and_without_the_no_data_message_raises():
    html = fixture_html("trade-quotes-holiday.html").replace("Looks like there is no data coming back.",
                                                             "Something else went wrong.")
    with pytest.raises(ParseError, match="no quotes table"):
        parse_trade_quotes(html, DAY)
    with pytest.raises(ParseError, match="trade-quotes"):
        parse_trade_quotes("<html><body>Down for maintenance</body></html>", DAY)


def test_no_data_message_ignores_case_and_line_breaks():
    assert is_no_data_page("<p>LOOKS like there is\n   no data coming back.</p>")
    assert not is_no_data_page("<p>Looks like there is data coming back.</p>")


def test_renamed_price_header_raises_even_when_indices_parse():
    html = (fixture_html("trade-quotes.html")
            .replace("Closing <br />Price <br />($)", "Close ($)").replace("Last <br />Traded <br />Price <br />($)", "Last ($)"))
    with pytest.raises(ParseError, match="no quotes table"):
        parse_trade_quotes(html, DAY)


def test_renamed_optional_header_is_warned(caplog):
    html = fixture_html("trade-quotes.html").replace("<th>Closing<br />Ask ($)</th><th>Volume</th>",
                                                     "<th>Closing<br />Ask ($)</th><th>Shares</th>")
    quotes, _ = parse_trade_quotes(html, DAY)
    assert quotes["9001"].volume is None
    assert "trade-quotes: column(s) not found, values will be null: volume" in caplog.messages


def test_rows_without_a_link_or_symbol_are_skipped_and_counted_once(caplog):
    html = fixture_html("trade-quotes.html").replace('title="Beta Capital Limited">BETA</a>',
                                                     'title="Beta Capital Limited"></a>')
    quotes, _ = parse_trade_quotes(html, DAY)
    assert set(quotes) == {"9001"}  # BETA has a link but no symbol; ZZZ has a symbol but no link
    assert caplog.messages == ["trade-quotes: skipped 2 row(s) without an instrument link or symbol"]


@pytest.mark.parametrize("close, change, expected", [
    (Decimal("12.50"), Decimal("-0.25"), Decimal("-1.96")),
    (Decimal("10"), Decimal("0"), Decimal("0.00")),
    (Decimal("1"), Decimal("1"), None),  # previous price 0
    (None, Decimal("1"), None),
    (Decimal("1"), None, None),
])
def test_compute_change_pct(close, change, expected):
    assert compute_change_pct(close, change) == expected
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Append the quotes and indices parser**

Append to the end of `jamstockex/parse.py`:

```python


# --- trade quotes and indices ---------------------------------------------

QUOTE_COLUMNS: Aliases = {
    "symbol": ("symbol",),
    "close": ("closingprice", "closeprice", "lasttradedprice", "lastprice"),
    "change": ("pricechange", "change", "changes"),
    "change_pct": ("pctchange", "changepct", "percentchange"),
    "volume": ("volume", "volumetraded"),
}

INDEX_COLUMNS: Aliases = {
    "name": ("index", "indexname"),
    "value": ("value", "indexvalue", "closingvalue"),
    "change": ("change", "pointchange", "valuechange"),
    "change_pct": ("pctchange", "changepct", "percentchange"),
    "volume": ("volume",),
}


# The site's message for a date with no trade data. Matched on squashed, lower-cased page text.
NO_DATA_MARKER = "looks like there is no data coming back"


def is_no_data_page(html: str) -> bool:
    return NO_DATA_MARKER in " ".join(html.lower().split())


def compute_change_pct(close: Decimal | None, change: Decimal | None) -> Decimal | None:
    if close is None or change is None:
        return None
    previous = close - change
    if previous == 0:
        return None
    return (change / previous * 100).quantize(Decimal("0.01"))


def parse_trade_quotes(html: str, trade_date: dt.date) -> tuple[dict[str, Quote], list[Index]]:
    page = extract_tables(html)
    quote_tables = matching_tables(page, QUOTE_COLUMNS, ("symbol", "close"))
    index_tables = [
        (table, cols)
        for table, cols in matching_tables(page, INDEX_COLUMNS, ("name", "value"))
        if "symbol" not in header_index(table)
    ]
    # A date with no trading (holiday) gets a page with no tables and the site's "no data" message.
    # No quotes table without that message means the layout changed, which must fail loudly
    # rather than look like a holiday.
    if not quote_tables:
        if is_no_data_page(html):
            log.info("trade-quotes: the site reports no data for %s (no trading that day)", trade_date.isoformat())
            return {}, []
        raise ParseError("trade-quotes", "no quotes table with 'symbol' and 'closing price' columns")
    if not index_tables:
        log.warning("trade-quotes: no indices table (with 'index' and 'value' columns) found")
    warn_missing_columns("trade-quotes", [cols for _, cols in quote_tables], ignore=("change_pct",))
    warn_missing_columns("trade-quotes (indices)", [cols for _, cols in index_tables])

    quotes: dict[str, Quote] = {}
    skipped: set[tuple[str, ...]] = set()  # a row repeated in the mobile copy counts once
    for table, cols in quote_tables:
        for row in data_rows(table):
            cells = row.cells
            code = row_instrument_code(row)
            if not code or not cell_text(cells, cols["symbol"]):
                skipped.add(tuple(cell.text for cell in cells))
                continue
            close = to_decimal(cell_text(cells, cols["close"]))
            change = to_decimal(cell_text(cells, cols["change"]))
            change_pct = to_decimal(cell_text(cells, cols["change_pct"]))
            if change_pct is None:
                change_pct = compute_change_pct(close, change)
            quotes.setdefault(code, Quote(
                date=trade_date,
                close=close,
                change=change,
                change_pct=change_pct,
                volume=to_int(cell_text(cells, cols["volume"])),
            ))

    if skipped:
        log.warning("trade-quotes: skipped %d row(s) without an instrument link or symbol", len(skipped))

    indices: dict[str, Index] = {}
    for table, cols in index_tables:
        for row in data_rows(table):
            cells = row.cells
            name = cell_text(cells, cols["name"])
            if not name:
                continue
            indices.setdefault(name, Index(
                name=name,
                date=trade_date,
                value=to_decimal(cell_text(cells, cols["value"])),
                change=to_decimal(cell_text(cells, cols["change"])),
                change_pct=to_decimal(cell_text(cells, cols["change_pct"])),
                volume=to_int(cell_text(cells, cols["volume"])),
            ))
    return quotes, list(indices.values())
```

### Task 9: Parser: corporate actions

**Why:** the corporate-actions page lists dividends, splits and other actions for all stocks. `type` is a required column and is normalised to lowercase snake_case; symbols are upper-cased; `"JMD 0.7500"` splits into currency and amount. Rows missing a symbol or a type are skipped and counted once. Duplicate rows, including the whole mobile copy of the table the site sends, collapse on the dedupe key `(symbol, type, record_date, ex_date, payment_date, amount, currency)`. On the real page every Instrument cell links to `?instrument=<numeric ID>`, so `instrument_code` usually comes straight from the page. The fixture copies the real headers (`Record Date`, `Instrument`, `Action`, `Ex-Date`, `Payment Date`, `Dividend Amount ($)`). v1 called the ex-date column "execution date", so that is an alias (superseded spec §3.2, §5, §6.2).

**Files:**
- Modify: `jamstockex/parse.py` (append)
- Test: `tests/test_parse_actions.py`, `tests/fixtures/corporate-actions.html`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: from `jamstockex/parse.py` (Task 6): `ParseError`, `Aliases`, `matching_tables`, `warn_missing_columns`, `data_rows`, `cell_text`, `row_instrument_code`, `parse_amount`, `parse_date`, `normalize_action_type`, `extract_tables`, `CorporateAction`.
- Produces:
  - `ACTION_COLUMNS: Aliases` (`symbol`, `type`, `amount` required; `record_date`, `ex_date`, `payment_date`)
  - `parse_corporate_actions(html: str) -> list[CorporateAction]`: `instrument_code` is set only when the row has an `?instrument=` link (otherwise `None`; Task 11 maps symbols to codes)

```python
import datetime as dt
from decimal import Decimal

import pytest

from jamstockex.models import CorporateAction
from jamstockex.parse import ParseError, parse_corporate_actions
from tests.helpers import fixture_html


def test_parses_dedupes_and_normalises(caplog):
    actions = parse_corporate_actions(fixture_html("corporate-actions.html"))
    assert actions == [
        CorporateAction(symbol="ACME", type="dividend", amount=Decimal("0.7500"), currency="JMD",
                        record_date=dt.date(2026, 11, 20), ex_date=dt.date(2026, 11, 18),
                        payment_date=dt.date(2026, 12, 4), instrument_code="9001"),
        CorporateAction(symbol="BETA", type="dividend", amount=Decimal("0.0100"), currency="USD",
                        record_date=dt.date(2026, 3, 2), ex_date=dt.date(2026, 2, 26),
                        payment_date=dt.date(2026, 3, 16), instrument_code="9002"),
        CorporateAction(symbol="ACME", type="stock_split", amount=None, currency=None,
                        record_date=dt.date(2026, 4, 14), ex_date=dt.date(2026, 4, 14),
                        payment_date=None, instrument_code="9001"),
    ]
    assert caplog.messages == []  # the mobile copy of the table adds nothing


def test_execution_date_header_is_an_ex_date_alias():
    html = fixture_html("corporate-actions.html").replace("<th>Ex-Date</th>", "<th>Execution Date</th>")
    assert parse_corporate_actions(html)[0].ex_date == dt.date(2026, 11, 18)


def test_missing_table_raises():
    with pytest.raises(ParseError, match="corporate-actions"):
        parse_corporate_actions("<html><body><table><tr><th>Foo</th></tr></table></body></html>")


def test_renamed_action_header_raises():
    html = fixture_html("corporate-actions.html").replace("<th>Action</th>", "<th>Kind</th>")
    with pytest.raises(ParseError, match="corporate-actions"):
        parse_corporate_actions(html)


def test_rows_without_an_action_type_are_skipped_and_counted(caplog):
    html = fixture_html("corporate-actions.html").replace("<td>Stock Split</td>", "<td></td>")
    actions = parse_corporate_actions(html)
    assert [a.type for a in actions] == ["dividend", "dividend"]
    assert caplog.messages == ["corporate-actions: skipped 1 row(s) without an instrument or action type"]
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Append the corporate-actions parser**

Append to the end of `jamstockex/parse.py`:

```python


# --- corporate actions ----------------------------------------------------

ACTION_COLUMNS: Aliases = {
    "symbol": ("instrument", "symbol", "security"),
    "type": ("action", "corporateaction", "actiontype"),
    "amount": ("dividendamount", "amount", "rate"),
    "record_date": ("recorddate",),
    "ex_date": ("exdate", "exdividenddate", "executiondate"),  # v1 called this column "execution date"
    "payment_date": ("paymentdate", "paymentduedate", "paymentdue", "payabledate"),
}


def parse_corporate_actions(html: str) -> list[CorporateAction]:
    page = extract_tables(html)
    tables = matching_tables(page, ACTION_COLUMNS, ("symbol", "type", "amount"))
    if not tables:
        raise ParseError("corporate-actions", "no table with 'instrument', 'action' and 'dividend amount' columns")
    warn_missing_columns("corporate-actions", [cols for _, cols in tables])
    actions: dict[tuple, CorporateAction] = {}
    skipped: set[tuple[str, ...]] = set()  # a row repeated in the mobile copy counts once
    for table, cols in tables:
        for row in data_rows(table):
            cells = row.cells
            symbol = cell_text(cells, cols["symbol"]).upper()
            action_type = normalize_action_type(cell_text(cells, cols["type"]))
            if not symbol or not action_type:
                skipped.add(tuple(cell.text for cell in cells))
                continue
            currency, amount = parse_amount(cell_text(cells, cols["amount"]))
            action = CorporateAction(
                symbol=symbol,
                type=action_type,
                amount=amount,
                currency=currency,
                record_date=parse_date(cell_text(cells, cols["record_date"])),
                ex_date=parse_date(cell_text(cells, cols["ex_date"])),
                payment_date=parse_date(cell_text(cells, cols["payment_date"])),
                instrument_code=row_instrument_code(row),
            )
            actions.setdefault(action.key(), action)
    if skipped:
        log.warning("corporate-actions: skipped %d row(s) without an instrument or action type", len(skipped))
    return list(actions.values())
```

### Task 10: Store: state, load, atomic save and the refresh lock

**Why:** `jse-data.json` is the app's only persistent store. `load` returns an empty state for a missing file; a corrupt file raises `StoreError` and is **never overwritten**. `save` writes a temp file in the same folder, fsyncs it, then `os.replace`s it, so a crash leaves either the old or the new file. `RefreshLock` is an exclusive `O_CREAT | O_EXCL` lock file holding the PID and time; a lock older than 10 minutes is taken over (spec §3.2, §6.1). This task creates `jamstockex/store.py`; Task 11 appends the merge rules.

**Files:**
- Create: `jamstockex/store.py` (state, I/O, lock; the module's imports already cover Task 11)
- Test: `tests/test_store_io.py`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `jamstockex.config.Config` (Task 2); `jamstockex.models.Company`, `CorporateAction`, `Index`, `Quote`, `isodate`, `parse_iso` (Task 3).
- Produces:
  - Constants: `DATA_FILE = "jse-data.json"`, `LOCK_FILE = "jse-data.lock"`, `FORMAT_VERSION = 1`, `DATASETS = ("listings", "quotes", "indices", "corporate_actions")`, `LOCK_STALE_AFTER_S = 600`
  - `class StoreError(Exception)` (message names the file and says it was left untouched); `class LockHeld(Exception)` (message ends `another refresh is running`)
  - `utc_timestamp(moment: datetime) -> str` (`YYYY-MM-DDTHH:MM:SSZ`); `parse_timestamp(text: str | None) -> datetime | None`
  - `@dataclass DatasetStatus(as_of: date | None = None, fetched_at: datetime | None = None, stale: bool = False, last_error: str | None = None, no_data_days: int = 0)` with `to_json`/`from_json`; `no_data_days` (used by quotes only, Task 11) is written only when above 0 and reads back as 0 when absent
  - `@dataclass State(companies: dict[str, Company], indices: list[Index], actions: list[CorporateAction], status: dict[str, DatasetStatus], markets: dict[str, DatasetStatus], unmatched: set[str], saved_at: datetime | None, blocked_close: datetime | None, last_failure_at: datetime | None)`. Every field has a default (`status` holds all four `DATASETS`); `markets` is keyed by market label.
  - `action_sort_key(action: CorporateAction) -> tuple` (record_date, then symbol); `state_to_json(state: State) -> dict[str, Any]`; `state_from_json(doc: dict[str, Any]) -> State`
  - `load(path: str | Path) -> State`; `save(state: State, path: str | Path, *, now: datetime) -> None` (creates the folder; sets `state.saved_at = now` only after the replace)
  - `class RefreshLock(path: str | Path, *, stale_after_s: float = 600, clock: Callable[[], float] = time.time)` with `acquire()`, `release()`, `held: bool`, and use as a context manager

```python
import datetime as dt
import json
import os
from decimal import Decimal

import pytest

from jamstockex import store
from jamstockex.models import Company, CorporateAction, Index, Quote

DAY = dt.date(2026, 11, 9)
NOW = dt.datetime(2026, 11, 9, 22, 30, 5, tzinfo=dt.timezone.utc)


def sample_state() -> store.State:
    state = store.State()
    state.companies = {
        "9001": Company(instrument_code="9001", symbol="ACME", name="Acme Holdings Limited",
                            market="Main Market", currency="JMD", listed_since=DAY,
                            quote=Quote(date=DAY, close=Decimal("12.50"), change=Decimal("-0.25"),
                                        change_pct=Decimal("-1.96"), volume=48213)),
        "9002": Company(instrument_code="9002", symbol="BETA", name="Beta Capital Limited",
                            market="USD Market", currency="USD", listed_since=DAY),
    }
    state.indices = [Index(name="JSE Main Index", date=DAY, value=Decimal("325456.78"), change=None,
                           change_pct=None, volume=None)]
    state.actions = [  # in saved order: by record_date
        CorporateAction(symbol="ZZZ", type="dividend", amount=Decimal("1"), currency="JMD",
                        record_date=dt.date(2026, 5, 1), ex_date=None, payment_date=None),
        CorporateAction(symbol="ACME", type="dividend", amount=Decimal("0.75"), currency="JMD",
                        record_date=dt.date(2026, 11, 20), ex_date=None, payment_date=None,
                        instrument_code="9001"),
    ]
    state.status["quotes"] = store.DatasetStatus(as_of=DAY, fetched_at=NOW, no_data_days=2)
    state.status["corporate_actions"] = store.DatasetStatus(stale=True, last_error="FetchError: boom")
    state.markets = {"Main Market": store.DatasetStatus(as_of=DAY, fetched_at=NOW)}
    state.unmatched = {"9003"}
    state.blocked_close = dt.datetime(2026, 11, 6, 22, 30, tzinfo=dt.timezone.utc)
    state.last_failure_at = NOW
    return state


def test_missing_file_gives_empty_state(tmp_path):
    state = store.load(tmp_path / "jse-data.json")
    assert state.companies == {} and state.indices == [] and state.actions == []
    assert state.markets == {} and state.unmatched == set()
    assert set(state.status) == set(store.DATASETS)
    assert all(s == store.DatasetStatus() for s in state.status.values())
    assert state.saved_at is None and state.blocked_close is None and state.last_failure_at is None


def test_save_then_load_round_trips(tmp_path):
    path = tmp_path / "data" / "jse-data.json"  # parent folder is created
    original = sample_state()
    store.save(original, path, now=NOW)
    assert original.saved_at == NOW
    loaded = store.load(path)
    assert loaded == original


def test_saved_file_is_sorted_utf8_json_with_a_format_version(tmp_path):
    path = tmp_path / "jse-data.json"
    store.save(sample_state(), path, now=NOW)
    doc = json.loads(path.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
    assert doc["format"] == store.FORMAT_VERSION
    assert doc["saved_at"] == "2026-11-09T22:30:05Z"
    assert [c["instrument_code"] for c in doc["companies"]] == ["9001", "9002"]
    assert [a["symbol"] for a in doc["corporate_actions"]] == ["ZZZ", "ACME"]  # by record_date
    assert doc["unmatched_codes"] == ["9003"]
    assert doc["blocked_close"] == "2026-11-06T22:30:00Z"
    assert doc["status"]["quotes"]["no_data_days"] == 2
    assert "no_data_days" not in doc["status"]["indices"]  # written only when > 0


@pytest.mark.parametrize("step", ["fsync", "replace"])
def test_a_crash_before_the_replace_leaves_the_old_file_intact(tmp_path, monkeypatch, step):
    path = tmp_path / "jse-data.json"
    store.save(sample_state(), path, now=NOW)
    before = path.read_bytes()

    def crash(*args, **kwargs):
        raise OSError("simulated crash")

    monkeypatch.setattr(store.os, step, crash)
    changed = sample_state()
    changed.companies.clear()
    with pytest.raises(OSError, match="simulated crash"):
        store.save(changed, path, now=NOW + dt.timedelta(days=1))
    assert path.read_bytes() == before
    assert [p.name for p in tmp_path.iterdir()] == ["jse-data.json"]  # temp file removed
    assert changed.saved_at is None


@pytest.mark.parametrize("text", [
    "{not json",
    '{"format": 1, "companies": "nope"}',
    '{"format": 99}',
    "[]",
])
def test_corrupt_file_raises_store_error_and_is_untouched(tmp_path, text):
    path = tmp_path / "jse-data.json"
    path.write_text(text, encoding="utf-8")
    with pytest.raises(store.StoreError) as caught:
        store.load(path)
    assert str(path) in str(caught.value)
    assert "left untouched" in str(caught.value)
    assert path.read_text(encoding="utf-8") == text


def test_lock_is_exclusive_and_released(tmp_path):
    path = tmp_path / "jse-data.lock"
    with store.RefreshLock(path):
        data = json.loads(path.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
        assert data["pid"] == os.getpid()
        with pytest.raises(store.LockHeld, match="another refresh is running"):
            store.RefreshLock(path).acquire()
    assert not path.exists()


def test_stale_lock_is_taken_over(tmp_path):
    path = tmp_path / "jse-data.lock"
    path.write_text(json.dumps({"pid": 1, "time": 1_000.0}), encoding="utf-8")
    with pytest.raises(store.LockHeld):
        store.RefreshLock(path, clock=lambda: 1_000.0 + 599).acquire()
    lock = store.RefreshLock(path, clock=lambda: 1_000.0 + 601)
    lock.acquire()
    assert json.loads(path.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))["time"] == 1_601.0
    lock.release()


def test_unreadable_lock_falls_back_to_its_modification_time(tmp_path):
    path = tmp_path / "jse-data.lock"
    path.write_text("garbage", encoding="utf-8")
    os.utime(path, (5_000.0, 5_000.0))
    with pytest.raises(store.LockHeld):
        store.RefreshLock(path, clock=lambda: 5_000.0 + 60).acquire()
    with store.RefreshLock(path, clock=lambda: 5_000.0 + 601):
        assert path.exists()
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the state, load, save and lock**

`jamstockex/store.py`:

```python
"""The local data file (jse-data.json): state, load, atomic save and the refresh lock.

- `load` returns an empty State when the file is missing. A corrupt file raises
  StoreError and is never overwritten.
- `save` writes a temp file in the same folder, fsyncs it, then os.replace()s it
  over the old file, so a crash leaves either the old or the new file intact.
- `RefreshLock` is an exclusive lock file (O_CREAT | O_EXCL) holding the PID and
  time; a lock older than 10 minutes is taken over.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import contextlib
import datetime as dt
import json
import logging
import os
import tempfile
import time
from dataclasses import dataclass, field, replace
from decimal import Decimal
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Any, Callable

from jamstockex.config import Config
from jamstockex.models import Company, CorporateAction, Index, Quote, isodate, parse_iso

log = logging.getLogger(__name__)

DATA_FILE = "jse-data.json"
LOCK_FILE = "jse-data.lock"
FORMAT_VERSION = 1
DATASETS = ("listings", "quotes", "indices", "corporate_actions")
LOCK_STALE_AFTER_S = 10 * 60


class StoreError(Exception):
    """The data file can't be read. It is left untouched."""


class LockHeld(Exception):
    """Another refresh holds the lock."""


def utc_timestamp(moment: dt.datetime) -> str:
    return moment.astimezone(dt.timezone.utc).strftime("%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ")


def parse_timestamp(text: str | None) -> dt.datetime | None:
    if text is None:
        return None
    return dt.datetime.fromisoformat(text.replace("Z", "+00:00")).astimezone(dt.timezone.utc)


def _timestamp_or_none(moment: dt.datetime | None) -> str | None:
    return utc_timestamp(moment) if moment else None


@dataclass
class DatasetStatus:
    as_of: dt.date | None = None
    fetched_at: dt.datetime | None = None
    stale: bool = False
    last_error: str | None = None
    # Quotes only: trading-day refreshes in a row that returned no quotes. Written only when > 0.
    no_data_days: int = 0

    def to_json(self) -> dict[str, Any]:
        doc = {
            "as_of": isodate(self.as_of),
            "fetched_at": _timestamp_or_none(self.fetched_at),
            "stale": self.stale,
            "last_error": self.last_error,
        }
        if self.no_data_days:
            doc["no_data_days"] = self.no_data_days
        return doc

    @classmethod
    def from_json(cls, d: dict[str, Any]) -> DatasetStatus:
        return cls(
            as_of=parse_iso(d.get("as_of")),
            fetched_at=parse_timestamp(d.get("fetched_at")),
            stale=bool(d.get("stale", False)),
            last_error=d.get("last_error"),
            no_data_days=int(d.get("no_data_days", 0)),
        )


def _empty_status() -> dict[str, DatasetStatus]:
    return {name: DatasetStatus() for name in DATASETS}


@dataclass
class State:
    companies: dict[str, Company] = field(default_factory=dict)
    indices: list[Index] = field(default_factory=list)
    actions: list[CorporateAction] = field(default_factory=list)
    status: dict[str, DatasetStatus] = field(default_factory=_empty_status)
    # Per listings market, keyed by market label (the value of Company.market).
    markets: dict[str, DatasetStatus] = field(default_factory=dict)
    # Quote codes that a full listings refresh didn't explain. They don't trigger another refresh.
    unmatched: set[str] = field(default_factory=set)
    saved_at: dt.datetime | None = None
    # The market close at which the site last blocked us; no automatic attempt until a later close.
    blocked_close: dt.datetime | None = None
    # When the last refresh had a (non-block) failure; automatic retries wait after it.
    last_failure_at: dt.datetime | None = None


def action_sort_key(action: CorporateAction) -> tuple:
    return (action.record_date or dt.date.min, action.symbol, action.type,
            action.payment_date or dt.date.min, action.ex_date or dt.date.min)


def state_to_json(state: State) -> dict[str, Any]:
    return {
        "format": FORMAT_VERSION,
        "saved_at": _timestamp_or_none(state.saved_at),
        "companies": [c.to_json() for c in sorted(state.companies.values(), key=lambda c: c.instrument_code)],
        "indices": [i.to_json() for i in sorted(state.indices, key=lambda i: i.name)],
        "corporate_actions": [a.to_json() for a in sorted(state.actions, key=action_sort_key)],
        "status": {name: s.to_json() for name, s in state.status.items()},
        "markets": {label: m.to_json() for label, m in sorted(state.markets.items())},
        "unmatched_codes": sorted(state.unmatched),
        "blocked_close": _timestamp_or_none(state.blocked_close),
        "last_failure_at": _timestamp_or_none(state.last_failure_at),
    }


def state_from_json(doc: dict[str, Any]) -> State:
    if doc.get("format") != FORMAT_VERSION:
        raise ValueError(f"unsupported format {doc.get('format')!r} (expected {FORMAT_VERSION})")
    state = State(
        companies={d["instrument_code"]: Company.from_json(d) for d in doc["companies"]},
        indices=[Index.from_json(d) for d in doc["indices"]],
        actions=[CorporateAction.from_json(d) for d in doc["corporate_actions"]],
        markets={label: DatasetStatus.from_json(d) for label, d in doc["markets"].items()},
        unmatched=set(doc["unmatched_codes"]),
        saved_at=parse_timestamp(doc.get("saved_at")),
        blocked_close=parse_timestamp(doc.get("blocked_close")),
        last_failure_at=parse_timestamp(doc.get("last_failure_at")),
    )
    for name, data in doc["status"].items():
        if name in state.status:
            state.status[name] = DatasetStatus.from_json(data)
    return state


def load(path: str | Path) -> State:
    path = Path(path)
    try:
        text = path.read_text(encoding="utf-8")
    except FileNotFoundError:
        return State()
    except (OSError, UnicodeDecodeError) as exc:
        raise StoreError(f"{path}: can't read the data file ({type(exc).__name__}: {exc}); it was left untouched") from exc
    try:
        return state_from_json(json.loads(text, parse_float=Decimal))
    except (ValueError, KeyError, TypeError, AttributeError) as exc:
        raise StoreError(f"{path}: not a valid jamstockex data file ({type(exc).__name__}: {exc}); "
                         "it was left untouched") from exc


def save(state: State, path: str | Path, *, now: dt.datetime) -> None:
    """Write atomically: temp file in the same folder, fsync, then os.replace."""
    path = Path(path)
    path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    doc = state_to_json(replace(state, saved_at=now))
    text = json.dumps(doc, indent=2, sort_keys=True, ensure_ascii=False) + "\n"
    fd, tmp = tempfile.mkstemp(dir=path.parent, prefix=f".{path.name}.", suffix=".tmp")
    try:
        with os.fdopen(fd, "w", encoding="utf-8") as handle:
            handle.write(text)
            handle.flush()
            os.fsync(handle.fileno())
        os.replace(tmp, path)
    except BaseException:
        with contextlib.suppress(FileNotFoundError):
            os.unlink(tmp)
        raise
    state.saved_at = now


class RefreshLock:
    """Exclusive lock file. Use as a context manager; raises LockHeld if another refresh holds it."""

    def __init__(self, path: str | Path, *, stale_after_s: float = LOCK_STALE_AFTER_S,
                 clock: Callable[[], float] = time.time) -> None:
        self.path = Path(path)
        self.stale_after_s = stale_after_s
        self.clock = clock
        self.held = False

    def acquire(self) -> None:
        self.path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
        for _ in range(2):
            try:
                fd = os.open(self.path, os.O_CREAT | os.O_EXCL | os.O_WRONLY, 0o644)
            except FileExistsError:
                if not self._is_stale():
                    raise LockHeld(f"{self.path}: another refresh is running") from None
                log.warning("Taking over a stale lock: %s", self.path)
                with contextlib.suppress(FileNotFoundError):
                    os.unlink(self.path)
                continue
            with os.fdopen(fd, "w", encoding="utf-8") as handle:
                json.dump({"pid": os.getpid(), "time": self.clock()}, handle)
            self.held = True
            return
        raise LockHeld(f"{self.path}: another refresh is running")

    def _is_stale(self) -> bool:
        try:
            started = float(json.loads(self.path.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))["time"])
        except (OSError, ValueError, KeyError, TypeError):
            try:
                started = self.path.stat().st_mtime
            except FileNotFoundError:
                return True
        return self.clock() - started > self.stale_after_s

    def release(self) -> None:
        if self.held:
            with contextlib.suppress(FileNotFoundError):
                os.unlink(self.path)
            self.held = False

    def __enter__(self) -> RefreshLock:
        self.acquire()
        return self

    def __exit__(self, *exc: object) -> None:
        self.release()
```

Run: `.venv/bin/pytest tests/test_store_io.py -q`
Expected: `12 passed`.

Then run the whole suite: `.venv/bin/pytest -q`
Expected: `149 passed`.

### Task 11: Store: merge rules

**Why:** folds one refresh's results into the stored state, applying the superseded spec §6.1–§6.2 rules unchanged:
- **Listings, per market:** upsert by `instrument_code` (new ones get `listed_since = today`). A company missing from its market's page is delisted only if that page loaded, is non-empty, and passes the integer guard `found × 100 ≥ (100 − max_listing_drop_pct) × previous`; otherwise the delisting is held with a warning. A company that moved market isn't delisted; a delisted code that reappears is relisted. Each market has its own status, and a failed market goes stale alone.
- **Quotes:** replace present rows and keep absent ones; unknown codes are skipped with a warning. After a full listings refresh, codes still unknown go to `unmatched_codes`, and they leave when found.
- **Indices** are replaced only when non-empty. **Corporate actions** are a union on the dedupe key and are never deleted. Symbols map to `instrument_code` when unique, then by currency; otherwise `null`.
- **Status:** `last_error` and `stale` are set on failure and cleared on success. A holiday (the site's "no data" page, Task 8) is a success with zero rows, so the previous quotes and their `as_of` are kept.
- **No-data safety net:** in case the "no data" page ever stands for a site change, `status.quotes.no_data_days` counts successful quotes refreshes in a row that returned no quotes. Any refresh with at least one quote resets it to 0; a failed quotes fetch leaves it alone. When it reaches `NO_DATA_STALE_DAYS` (3), quotes and indices are marked stale with `last_error` `"No trade data for 3 trading days in a row; the site may have changed"`, and the message is returned as a warning. It is not a failure: the run isn't blocked, there's no failure wait, and the next close is fetched as usual.

**Files:**
- Modify: `jamstockex/store.py` (append)
- Test: `tests/test_store_merge.py`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: from `jamstockex/store.py` (Task 10): `State`, `DatasetStatus`; `jamstockex.config.Config`, `default_config` (Task 2); the models (Task 3).
- Produces:
  - `@dataclass ScrapeResult(trade_date: date, listings: dict[str, list[Company]] | None = None, listing_errors: dict[str, str] = {}, listings_full: bool = False, quotes: dict[str, Quote] | None = None, indices: list[Index] | None = None, actions: list[CorporateAction] | None = None, errors: dict[str, str] = {})`. The dict defaults use `field(default_factory=dict)`. A field left `None` and absent from `errors` was not attempted. `listings` and `listing_errors` are keyed by market label; `errors` by dataset name (`listings`, `quotes`, `indices`, `corporate_actions`).
  - `@dataclass MergeStats(companies_added: int = 0, companies_updated: int = 0, companies_delisted: int = 0, actions_added: int = 0)`
  - `NO_DATA_STALE_DAYS = 3`
  - `merge(state: State, result: ScrapeResult, *, today: date, now: datetime, settings: Config, stats: MergeStats | None = None) -> list[str]`: mutates `state` (including `status["quotes"].no_data_days`), returns warnings (and logs them), fills `stats`

```python
import datetime as dt
from decimal import Decimal

from dataclasses import replace

from jamstockex import store
from jamstockex.config import default_config
from jamstockex.models import Company, CorporateAction, Index, Quote
from jamstockex.store import ScrapeResult, State

TODAY = dt.date(2026, 11, 9)
NOW = dt.datetime(2026, 11, 9, 22, 30, tzinfo=dt.timezone.utc)
EARLIER = dt.date(2026, 11, 2)


SYMBOLS = {"9001": "ACME", "9002": "BETA", "9003": "GAMMA"}  # instrument_code -> symbol, for the defaults


def company(code, symbol=None, currency="JMD", market="Main Market", **kw):
    return Company(instrument_code=code, symbol=symbol or SYMBOLS.get(code, f"S{code}"), name=f"{code} Limited",
                   market=market, currency=currency, **kw)


def quote(close, day=TODAY):
    return Quote(date=day, close=Decimal(close), change=Decimal("0"), change_pct=Decimal("0"), volume=100)


def action(symbol, amount="0.75", currency="JMD", type_="dividend", code=None, record=dt.date(2026, 11, 20)):
    return CorporateAction(symbol=symbol, type=type_, amount=Decimal(amount) if amount else None,
                           currency=currency, record_date=record, ex_date=None, payment_date=None,
                           instrument_code=code)


def settings(drop=20):
    defaults = default_config()
    return replace(defaults, refresh=replace(defaults.refresh, max_listing_drop_pct=drop))


def merge(state, result, drop=20):
    return store.merge(state, result, today=TODAY, now=NOW, settings=settings(drop))


def refresh(pages, errors=None, full=True, **kw):
    """A ScrapeResult for a listings refresh. pages: market label -> companies."""
    return ScrapeResult(trade_date=TODAY, listings=pages, listing_errors=errors or {}, listings_full=full, **kw)


def listed_state(*companies):
    state = State()
    for c in companies:
        c.listed_since = EARLIER
        state.companies[c.instrument_code] = c
        state.markets[c.market] = store.DatasetStatus(as_of=EARLIER)
    state.status["listings"] = store.DatasetStatus(as_of=EARLIER)
    return state


def main_market(*codes):
    return [company(code) for code in codes]


# --- listings -------------------------------------------------------------

def test_new_listings_get_listed_since_today_and_status_updates():
    state = State()
    merge(state, refresh({"Main Market": main_market("9001")}))
    assert state.companies["9001"].listed_since == TODAY
    fresh = store.DatasetStatus(as_of=TODAY, fetched_at=NOW, stale=False, last_error=None)
    assert state.status["listings"] == fresh
    assert state.markets == {"Main Market": fresh}


def test_listing_update_keeps_listed_since_and_quote():
    state = listed_state(*main_market("9001"))
    state.companies["9001"].quote = quote("10")
    renamed = company("9001", sector="Retail")
    renamed.name = "Acme Group Limited"
    merge(state, refresh({"Main Market": [renamed]}))
    acme = state.companies["9001"]
    assert acme.name == "Acme Group Limited" and acme.sector == "Retail"
    assert acme.listed_since == EARLIER
    assert acme.quote == quote("10")


def test_missing_instrument_is_delisted():
    state = listed_state(*main_market(*[f"{8000 + i}" for i in range(10)]))
    warnings = merge(state, refresh({"Main Market": main_market(*[f"{8000 + i}" for i in range(9)])}))
    gone = state.companies["8009"]
    assert gone.listed is False and gone.delisted_on == TODAY
    assert warnings == []


def test_failed_market_keeps_its_instruments_and_only_it_goes_stale():
    state = listed_state(company("9001"), company("9004", market="Junior Market"))
    merge(state, refresh({"Main Market": main_market("9001")},
                         errors={"Junior Market": "FetchError: boom"}))
    assert state.companies["9004"].listed is True
    assert state.markets["Main Market"].stale is False and state.markets["Main Market"].as_of == TODAY
    assert state.markets["Junior Market"].stale is True
    assert state.markets["Junior Market"].as_of == EARLIER  # not advanced -> retried next run
    assert state.status["listings"].stale is True
    assert state.status["listings"].last_error == "Junior Market: FetchError: boom"
    assert state.status["listings"].as_of == EARLIER  # oldest market refresh


def test_market_recovers_and_listings_stop_being_stale():
    state = listed_state(company("9001"), company("9004", market="Junior Market"))
    merge(state, refresh({"Main Market": main_market("9001")}, errors={"Junior Market": "FetchError: boom"}))
    merge(state, refresh({"Junior Market": [company("9004", market="Junior Market")]}, full=False))
    assert state.status["listings"] == store.DatasetStatus(as_of=TODAY, fetched_at=NOW, stale=False, last_error=None)


def test_delisting_held_when_a_market_drops_too_far():
    state = listed_state(*main_market(*[f"{8000 + i}" for i in range(10)]))
    warnings = merge(state, refresh({"Main Market": main_market(*[f"{8000 + i}" for i in range(7)])}))  # 7 < 8
    assert all(c.listed for c in state.companies.values())
    assert any("Delisting held for Main Market" in w for w in warnings)


def test_delisting_boundary_is_inclusive_and_exact():
    state = listed_state(*main_market(*[f"{8000 + i}" for i in range(90)]))
    merge(state, refresh({"Main Market": main_market(*[f"{8000 + i}" for i in range(63)])}), drop=30)  # exactly 70%
    assert sum(1 for c in state.companies.values() if not c.listed) == 27


def test_empty_page_for_a_small_market_does_not_delist_it():
    mains = main_market(*[f"{7000 + i}" for i in range(100)])
    usd = [company(f"{7500 + i}", currency="USD", market="USD Market") for i in range(10)]
    state = listed_state(*mains, *usd)
    warnings = merge(state, refresh({"Main Market": main_market(*[f"{7000 + i}" for i in range(100)]),
                                     "USD Market": []}))
    assert all(c.listed for c in state.companies.values())
    assert warnings == ["Delisting held for USD Market: 0 instrument(s) on the page vs 10 previously listed "
                        "(max drop 20%)"]


def test_instrument_moving_market_is_not_delisted():
    state = listed_state(company("9001", market="Junior Market"))
    merge(state, refresh({"Main Market": main_market("9001"), "Junior Market": []}))
    assert state.companies["9001"].listed is True
    assert state.companies["9001"].market == "Main Market"


def test_relisting_clears_delisted_on():
    state = listed_state(*main_market("9001"))
    state.companies["9001"].listed = False
    state.companies["9001"].delisted_on = EARLIER
    merge(state, refresh({"Main Market": main_market("9001")}))
    assert state.companies["9001"].listed is True
    assert state.companies["9001"].delisted_on is None
    assert state.companies["9001"].listed_since == EARLIER


def test_listings_not_attempted_leaves_status_alone():
    state = listed_state(*main_market("9001"))
    merge(state, ScrapeResult(trade_date=TODAY))
    assert state.status["listings"] == store.DatasetStatus(as_of=EARLIER)
    assert state.markets == {"Main Market": store.DatasetStatus(as_of=EARLIER)}


def test_full_refresh_remembers_unmatched_quote_codes_until_they_appear():
    state = listed_state(*main_market("9001"))
    merge(state, refresh({"Main Market": main_market("9001")},
                         quotes={"9001": quote("1"), "9003": quote("2")}, indices=[]))
    assert state.unmatched == {"9003"}
    merge(state, refresh({"Main Market": main_market("9001", "9003")}, full=False))
    assert state.unmatched == set()


def test_holiday_full_refresh_keeps_recorded_unmatched_codes():
    state = listed_state(*main_market("9001"))
    state.unmatched = {"9003"}
    merge(state, refresh({"Main Market": main_market("9001")}, quotes={}, indices=[]))
    assert state.unmatched == {"9003"}


def test_partial_refresh_does_not_record_unmatched_codes():
    state = listed_state(*main_market("9001"))
    merge(state, refresh({"Main Market": main_market("9001")}, full=False,
                         quotes={"9003": quote("2")}, indices=[]))
    assert state.unmatched == set()


# --- quotes and indices ---------------------------------------------------

def test_quotes_replace_present_and_keep_absent():
    state = listed_state(*main_market("9001", "9004"))
    state.companies["9004"].quote = quote("5", day=EARLIER)
    merge(state, ScrapeResult(trade_date=TODAY, quotes={"9001": quote("12")}, indices=[]))
    assert state.companies["9001"].quote == quote("12")
    assert state.companies["9004"].quote == quote("5", day=EARLIER)
    assert state.status["quotes"].as_of == TODAY and state.status["quotes"].stale is False


def test_unknown_quote_is_skipped_with_warning():
    state = listed_state(*main_market("9001"))
    warnings = merge(state, ScrapeResult(trade_date=TODAY, quotes={"9003": quote("1")}, indices=[]))
    assert "9003" not in state.companies
    assert any("9003" in w for w in warnings)


def test_holiday_keeps_quotes_indices_and_as_of():
    state = listed_state(*main_market("9001"))
    state.companies["9001"].quote = quote("10", day=EARLIER)
    old_index = Index(name="JSE Main Index", date=EARLIER, value=Decimal("1"), change=None, change_pct=None, volume=None)
    state.indices = [old_index]
    state.status["quotes"] = store.DatasetStatus(as_of=EARLIER)
    state.status["indices"] = store.DatasetStatus(as_of=EARLIER)
    merge(state, ScrapeResult(trade_date=TODAY, quotes={}, indices=[]))
    assert state.companies["9001"].quote.date == EARLIER
    assert state.indices == [old_index]
    assert state.status["quotes"].as_of == EARLIER and state.status["quotes"].stale is False

def test_indices_replaced_when_present():
    state = State()
    new_index = Index(name="JSE Main Index", date=TODAY, value=Decimal("2"), change=None, change_pct=None, volume=None)
    merge(state, ScrapeResult(trade_date=TODAY, quotes={}, indices=[new_index]))
    assert state.indices == [new_index]
    assert state.status["indices"].as_of == TODAY


def test_failure_marks_stale_then_success_clears():
    state = listed_state(*main_market("9001"))
    state.status["quotes"] = store.DatasetStatus(as_of=EARLIER, fetched_at=NOW - dt.timedelta(days=7))
    merge(state, ScrapeResult(trade_date=TODAY, errors={"quotes": "BlockedError: 403", "indices": "BlockedError: 403"}))
    assert state.status["quotes"].stale is True
    assert state.status["quotes"].last_error == "BlockedError: 403"
    assert state.status["quotes"].as_of == EARLIER
    merge(state, ScrapeResult(trade_date=TODAY, quotes={"9001": quote("1")}, indices=[]))
    assert state.status["quotes"] == store.DatasetStatus(as_of=TODAY, fetched_at=NOW, stale=False, last_error=None)


NO_DATA = "No trade data for 3 trading days in a row; the site may have changed"


def no_trading():
    return ScrapeResult(trade_date=TODAY, quotes={}, indices=[])


def test_days_without_quotes_are_counted_and_any_quote_resets_the_count():
    state = listed_state(*main_market("9001"))
    merge(state, no_trading())
    merge(state, no_trading())
    assert state.status["quotes"].no_data_days == 2
    assert state.status["quotes"].stale is False
    merge(state, ScrapeResult(trade_date=TODAY, quotes={"9001": quote("1")}, indices=[]))
    assert state.status["quotes"].no_data_days == 0


def test_a_failed_quotes_fetch_leaves_the_count_alone():
    state = listed_state(*main_market("9001"))
    state.status["quotes"].no_data_days = 2
    merge(state, ScrapeResult(trade_date=TODAY, errors={"quotes": "FetchError: x", "indices": "FetchError: x"}))
    assert state.status["quotes"].no_data_days == 2


def test_third_day_without_quotes_marks_quotes_and_indices_stale():
    state = listed_state(*main_market("9001"))
    warnings = [merge(state, no_trading()) for _ in range(3)]
    assert warnings == [[], [], [NO_DATA]]
    for name in ("quotes", "indices"):
        assert state.status[name].stale is True
        assert state.status[name].last_error == NO_DATA
    assert state.status["quotes"].fetched_at == NOW  # a successful fetch: the next close is fetched as usual
    merge(state, ScrapeResult(trade_date=TODAY, quotes={"9001": quote("1")}, indices=[]))
    assert state.status["quotes"] == store.DatasetStatus(as_of=TODAY, fetched_at=NOW)
    assert state.status["indices"].stale is False and state.status["indices"].last_error is None

    state.actions = [action("ACME", amount="0.75")]
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Append the merge rules**

Append to the end of `jamstockex/store.py`:

```python




# --- merge ----------------------------------------------------------------


NO_DATA_STALE_DAYS = 3  # trading days in a row without quotes before quotes and indices are marked stale


@dataclass
class ScrapeResult:
    """What one refresh fetched. A field left as None (and absent from errors) was not attempted."""

    trade_date: dt.date
    listings: dict[str, list[Company]] | None = None  # market label -> companies, for pages that loaded
    listing_errors: dict[str, str] = field(default_factory=dict)  # market label -> error
    listings_full: bool = False  # every configured market was requested this run
    quotes: dict[str, Quote] | None = None
    indices: list[Index] | None = None
    actions: list[CorporateAction] | None = None
    errors: dict[str, str] = field(default_factory=dict)


@dataclass
class MergeStats:
    """What a merge changed, for the run summary."""

    companies_added: int = 0
    companies_updated: int = 0
    companies_delisted: int = 0
    actions_added: int = 0


def merge(
    state: State,
    result: ScrapeResult,
    *,
    today: dt.date,
    now: dt.datetime,
    settings: Config,
    stats: MergeStats | None = None,
) -> list[str]:
    """Fold a scrape into `state`. Returns warnings; fills `stats` (if given) with change counts."""
    warnings: list[str] = []
    errors = result.errors
    if stats is None:
        stats = MergeStats()

    if result.listings is not None or result.listing_errors:
        warnings += _merge_listings(state, result.listings or {}, result.listing_errors, today=today, now=now,
                                    max_drop_pct=settings.refresh.max_listing_drop_pct, stats=stats)
        if result.listings_full and result.quotes is not None:
            # Whatever a full refresh couldn't explain won't trigger another refresh tomorrow. Codes
            # already recorded stay until a listings refresh finds them (a holiday has no quotes).
            state.unmatched = (state.unmatched | set(result.quotes)) - set(state.companies)

    if result.quotes is not None or "quotes" in errors:
        if result.quotes is not None:
            warnings += _merge_quotes(state, result.quotes)
        _mark(state.status["quotes"], errors.get("quotes"), now, as_of=result.trade_date if result.quotes else None)

    if result.indices is not None or "indices" in errors:
        if result.indices:
            state.indices = list(result.indices)
        _mark(state.status["indices"], errors.get("indices"), now,
              as_of=result.trade_date if result.indices else None)

    if result.quotes is not None and "quotes" not in errors:
        warning = _count_no_data_days(state, quotes_found=bool(result.quotes))
        if warning:
            warnings.append(warning)

    if result.actions is not None or "corporate_actions" in errors:
        if result.actions is not None:
            stats.actions_added += _merge_actions(state, result.actions)
        _mark(state.status["corporate_actions"], errors.get("corporate_actions"), now, as_of=result.trade_date)

    state.unmatched -= set(state.companies)
    _assign_instrument_codes(state)
    for warning in warnings:
        log.warning(warning)
    return warnings


def _mark(status: DatasetStatus, error: str | None, now: dt.datetime, *, as_of: dt.date | None) -> None:
    if error:
        status.stale = True
        status.last_error = error
        return
    status.stale = False
    status.last_error = None
    status.fetched_at = now
    if as_of is not None:
        status.as_of = as_of


def _count_no_data_days(state: State, *, quotes_found: bool) -> str | None:
    """Safety net for the holiday rule: a "no data" page every trading day may be a site change.

    Counts successful quotes refreshes in a row that returned no quotes; any quote resets it.
    At NO_DATA_STALE_DAYS, quotes and indices are marked stale. That is not a failure: there is
    no failure wait, and the next close is fetched as usual.
    """
    status = state.status["quotes"]
    status.no_data_days = 0 if quotes_found else status.no_data_days + 1
    if status.no_data_days < NO_DATA_STALE_DAYS:
        return None
    message = (f"No trade data for {status.no_data_days} trading days in a row; "
               "the site may have changed")
    for name in ("quotes", "indices"):
        state.status[name].stale = True
        state.status[name].last_error = message
    return message


def _merge_listings(state: State, pages: dict[str, list[Company]], failed: dict[str, str], *,
                    today: dt.date, now: dt.datetime, max_drop_pct: int, stats: MergeStats) -> list[str]:
    warnings: list[str] = []
    seen = {c.instrument_code for companies in pages.values() for c in companies}
    listed_before = {
        label: {code for code, c in state.companies.items() if c.listed and c.market == label} for label in pages
    }
    for companies in pages.values():
        for new in companies:
            old = state.companies.get(new.instrument_code)
            if old is None:
                new.listed, new.listed_since, new.delisted_on = True, today, None
                state.companies[new.instrument_code] = new
                stats.companies_added += 1
                continue
            before = (old.symbol, old.name, old.market, old.currency, old.sector, old.type, old.website,
                      old.listed, old.delisted_on)
            old.symbol, old.name, old.market = new.symbol, new.name, new.market
            old.currency, old.sector, old.type, old.website = new.currency, new.sector, new.type, new.website
            old.listed, old.delisted_on = True, None
            after = (old.symbol, old.name, old.market, old.currency, old.sector, old.type, old.website,
                     old.listed, old.delisted_on)
            if after != before:
                stats.companies_updated += 1

    # Delist per market, and only for markets whose page loaded. Each market has its own guard,
    # so an empty or truncated page for a small market can't delist it wholesale.
    for label in sorted(pages):
        previous = listed_before[label]
        missing = previous - seen
        if missing:
            found = len({c.instrument_code for c in pages[label]})
            if found == 0 or found * 100 < (100 - max_drop_pct) * len(previous):  # integer-safe
                warnings.append(
                    f"Delisting held for {label}: {found} instrument(s) on the page vs {len(previous)} "
                    f"previously listed (max drop {max_drop_pct}%)"
                )
            else:
                for code in sorted(missing):
                    state.companies[code].listed = False
                    state.companies[code].delisted_on = today
                stats.companies_delisted += len(missing)
                log.info("Delisted %d %s instrument(s): %s", len(missing), label, ", ".join(sorted(missing)))
        _mark(state.markets.setdefault(label, DatasetStatus()), None, now, as_of=today)

    for label, error in failed.items():
        _mark(state.markets.setdefault(label, DatasetStatus()), error, now, as_of=None)

    # The listings dataset summarises its markets. as_of is the oldest successful refresh.
    summary = state.status["listings"]
    stale = sorted(label for label, m in state.markets.items() if m.stale)
    summary.stale = bool(stale)
    summary.last_error = "; ".join(f"{label}: {state.markets[label].last_error}" for label in stale) or None
    if pages:
        summary.fetched_at = now
    dates = [m.as_of for m in state.markets.values() if m.as_of]
    summary.as_of = min(dates) if dates else None
    return warnings


def _merge_quotes(state: State, quotes: dict[str, Quote]) -> list[str]:
    unknown = []
    for code, new_quote in quotes.items():
        company = state.companies.get(code)
        if company is None:
            unknown.append(code)
            continue
        company.quote = new_quote
    if unknown:
        return [f"Quotes skipped for {len(unknown)} unknown instrument(s): {', '.join(sorted(unknown))}"]
    return []


def _merge_actions(state: State, actions: list[CorporateAction]) -> int:
    """Union with the stored actions. Returns how many were new."""
    existing = {a.key() for a in state.actions}
    added = 0
    for new in actions:
        if new.key() not in existing:
            state.actions.append(new)
            existing.add(new.key())
            added += 1
    return added


def _assign_instrument_codes(state: State) -> None:
    by_symbol: dict[str, list[Company]] = {}
    for company in state.companies.values():
        by_symbol.setdefault(company.symbol.upper(), []).append(company)
    state.actions = [
        a if a.instrument_code else replace(a, instrument_code=_match_code(a, by_symbol))
        for a in state.actions
    ]


def _match_code(action: CorporateAction, by_symbol: dict[str, list[Company]]) -> str | None:
    candidates = by_symbol.get(action.symbol.upper(), [])
    if len(candidates) == 1:
        return candidates[0].instrument_code
    if action.currency:
        same_currency = [c for c in candidates if (c.currency or "").upper() == action.currency.upper()]
        if len(same_currency) == 1:
            return same_currency[0].instrument_code
    return None
```

### Task 12: Refresh: when a refresh is due

**Why:** decides, with a clock comparison and no network traffic, what a refresh would fetch now (spec §6.1):
- `latest_close(now)` is the most recent weekday `D` (Jamaica time) whose `D close_time` is ≤ `now`: before 17:30 on a weekday that is the previous weekday; on a weekend it is Friday. Its date is the trade date requested.
- Quotes (with indices) and corporate actions are each due when their `fetched_at` is before the latest close, so there is at most one successful refresh per close. A holiday counts as a successful fetch.
- Listings follow the superseded spec §3.3: all markets on `--listings`, with no stored listings, or for an unknown quote code not already in `unmatched_codes`; a market alone when its last fetch failed or its last refresh is ≥ `listings_every_days` old.
- The corporate-actions window is `backfill_from` → `today + forward` until the first success, then `max(backfill_from, as_of − window)` → `today + forward`.
- Automatic attempts wait `retry_after_failure_minutes` after a failure, and after a block until the next close.

This task creates `jamstockex/refresh.py`; Task 13 appends the refresh run.

**Files:**
- Create: `jamstockex/refresh.py` (due logic; the module's imports already cover Task 13)
- Test: `tests/test_refresh_due.py`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `jamstockex.store.State`, `DatasetStatus` (Task 10); `jamstockex.config.JAMAICA`, `Config`, `default_config` (Task 2); `jamstockex.models.Quote`, `Company` (Task 3); `jamstockex.fetch.FetchError` (Task 4); the three page parsers and `ParseError` (Tasks 6–9).
- Produces:
  - Request paths (no host): `QUOTES_PATH = "/trading/trade-quotes/?market={market}&date={date}"`, `LISTINGS_PATH = "/listings/listed-companies/?market={market}"`, `ACTIONS_PATH = "/trading/corporate-actions/?instrumentCode=all-stocks&fromDate={from_date}&thruDate={thru_date}"`
  - `latest_close(now: datetime, close_time: time = time(17, 30)) -> datetime` (aware, in `JAMAICA`); `next_close_after(close: datetime, close_time: time = time(17, 30)) -> datetime`
  - `listings_due(state: State, quotes: dict[str, Quote] | None, markets: dict[int, str], today: date, every_days: int, force: bool) -> tuple[list[int], str | None]` (market codes in config order, and the reason)
  - `actions_window(status: DatasetStatus, today: date, settings: Config) -> tuple[date, date]`
  - `@dataclass(frozen=True) Due(close: datetime, today: date, quotes: bool, corporate_actions: bool, listings: tuple[int, ...], listings_reason: str | None, not_before: datetime | None)` with properties `trade_date -> date` and `anything -> bool`, and `automatic_allowed(now: datetime) -> bool`
  - `due(state: State, now: datetime, settings: Config, *, force_listings: bool = False) -> Due`

```python
import datetime as dt

import pytest

from jamstockex import refresh, store
from jamstockex.config import JAMAICA, default_config
from jamstockex.models import Company

SETTINGS = default_config()


def jm(year, month, day, hour=0, minute=0):
    return dt.datetime(year, month, day, hour, minute, tzinfo=JAMAICA)


@pytest.mark.parametrize("now, expected", [
    (jm(2026, 11, 10, 17, 29), jm(2026, 11, 9, 17, 30)),   # Tuesday before the close -> Monday
    (jm(2026, 11, 10, 17, 30), jm(2026, 11, 10, 17, 30)),  # Tuesday at the close
    (jm(2026, 11, 10, 23, 0), jm(2026, 11, 10, 17, 30)),   # Tuesday evening
    (jm(2026, 11, 13, 22, 0), jm(2026, 11, 13, 17, 30)),   # Friday night
    (jm(2026, 11, 14, 12, 0), jm(2026, 11, 13, 17, 30)),   # Saturday -> Friday
    (jm(2026, 11, 15, 20, 0), jm(2026, 11, 13, 17, 30)),   # Sunday -> Friday
    (jm(2026, 11, 16, 8, 0), jm(2026, 11, 13, 17, 30)),    # Monday morning -> Friday
])
def test_latest_close(now, expected):
    assert refresh.latest_close(now) == expected


def test_latest_close_accepts_utc_and_a_custom_close_time():
    now = dt.datetime(2026, 11, 10, 21, 0, tzinfo=dt.timezone.utc)  # 16:00 in Jamaica
    assert refresh.latest_close(now, dt.time(15, 0)) == jm(2026, 11, 10, 15, 0)
    assert refresh.latest_close(now) == jm(2026, 11, 9, 17, 30)


def test_next_close_after_skips_the_weekend():
    assert refresh.next_close_after(jm(2026, 11, 13, 17, 30)) == jm(2026, 11, 16, 17, 30)
    assert refresh.next_close_after(jm(2026, 11, 10, 17, 30)) == jm(2026, 11, 11, 17, 30)


def listed_state():
    state = store.State()
    for code, label in SETTINGS.markets.items():
        state.companies[f"{code}00"] = Company(instrument_code=f"{code}00", symbol=f"C{code}",
                                                  name="X", market=label)
        state.markets[label] = store.DatasetStatus(as_of=dt.date(2026, 11, 9), fetched_at=jm(2026, 11, 9, 18))
    return state


def fetched(state, moment, *names):
    for name in names:
        state.status[name] = store.DatasetStatus(as_of=moment.date(), fetched_at=moment)


def test_first_run_everything_is_due():
    plan = refresh.due(store.State(), jm(2026, 11, 10, 18), SETTINGS)
    assert plan.quotes and plan.corporate_actions
    assert plan.listings == (31, 22, 51, 49) and plan.listings_reason == "no stored listings"
    assert plan.trade_date == dt.date(2026, 11, 10)
    assert plan.not_before is None and plan.automatic_allowed(jm(2026, 11, 10, 18))


def test_at_most_one_successful_refresh_per_close():
    state = listed_state()
    fetched(state, jm(2026, 11, 10, 18), "quotes", "corporate_actions")
    for later in (jm(2026, 11, 10, 23), jm(2026, 11, 11, 9), jm(2026, 11, 11, 17, 29)):
        plan = refresh.due(state, later, SETTINGS)
        assert not plan.anything
        assert not plan.automatic_allowed(later)
    assert refresh.due(state, jm(2026, 11, 11, 17, 30), SETTINGS).quotes


def test_a_holiday_fetch_counts_as_the_fetch_for_that_close():
    state = listed_state()
    state.status["quotes"] = store.DatasetStatus(as_of=dt.date(2026, 10, 16), fetched_at=jm(2026, 10, 19, 18))
    state.status["corporate_actions"] = store.DatasetStatus(as_of=dt.date(2026, 10, 19), fetched_at=jm(2026, 10, 19, 18))
    assert not refresh.due(state, jm(2026, 10, 19, 20), SETTINGS).anything


def test_corporate_actions_are_due_on_their_own():
    state = listed_state()
    fetched(state, jm(2026, 11, 10, 18), "quotes")
    plan = refresh.due(state, jm(2026, 11, 10, 20), SETTINGS)
    assert not plan.quotes and plan.corporate_actions


def test_retry_waits_after_a_failure():
    state = listed_state()
    state.last_failure_at = jm(2026, 11, 10, 18)
    plan = refresh.due(state, jm(2026, 11, 10, 18, 30), SETTINGS)
    assert plan.quotes and plan.not_before == jm(2026, 11, 10, 19)
    assert not plan.automatic_allowed(jm(2026, 11, 10, 18, 30))
    assert refresh.due(state, jm(2026, 11, 10, 19), SETTINGS).automatic_allowed(jm(2026, 11, 10, 19))


def test_no_automatic_attempt_after_a_block_until_the_next_close():
    state = listed_state()
    state.blocked_close = jm(2026, 11, 13, 17, 30)  # blocked on Friday evening
    for moment in (jm(2026, 11, 13, 22), jm(2026, 11, 14, 12), jm(2026, 11, 16, 17, 29)):
        plan = refresh.due(state, moment, SETTINGS)
        assert plan.quotes and plan.not_before == jm(2026, 11, 16, 17, 30)
        assert not plan.automatic_allowed(moment)
    assert refresh.due(state, jm(2026, 11, 16, 17, 30), SETTINGS).automatic_allowed(jm(2026, 11, 16, 17, 30))


def test_listings_due_by_age_and_for_a_failed_market_alone():
    state = listed_state()
    fetched(state, jm(2026, 11, 10, 18), "quotes", "corporate_actions")
    state.markets["Bond Market"].stale = True
    plan = refresh.due(state, jm(2026, 11, 10, 20), SETTINGS)
    assert plan.listings == (51,) and plan.listings_reason == "retrying failed market(s): Bond Market"
    state.markets["Bond Market"].stale = False
    plan = refresh.due(state, jm(2026, 11, 16, 20), SETTINGS)  # 7 days after 2026-11-09
    assert plan.listings == (31, 22, 51, 49) and plan.listings_reason == "last refresh 2026-11-09"


def test_listings_due_for_unknown_quote_codes_but_not_unmatched_ones():
    state = listed_state()
    today = dt.date(2026, 11, 10)
    codes, reason = refresh.listings_due(state, {"9006": None}, SETTINGS.markets, today, 7, False)
    assert codes == [31, 22, 51, 49] and reason == "1 unknown instrument(s) in quotes"
    state.unmatched = {"9006"}
    assert refresh.listings_due(state, {"9006": None}, SETTINGS.markets, today, 7, False) == ([], None)
    assert refresh.listings_due(state, None, SETTINGS.markets, today, 7, True) == ([31, 22, 51, 49], "requested")


def test_corporate_actions_window_backfills_then_catches_up():
    today = dt.date(2026, 12, 24)
    first = refresh.actions_window(store.DatasetStatus(), today, SETTINGS)
    assert first == (dt.date(2026, 1, 1), dt.date(2027, 12, 24))
    after_outage = refresh.actions_window(store.DatasetStatus(as_of=dt.date(2026, 11, 9)), today, SETTINGS)
    assert after_outage == (dt.date(2026, 10, 10), dt.date(2027, 12, 24))
    early = refresh.actions_window(store.DatasetStatus(as_of=dt.date(2026, 1, 15)), today, SETTINGS)
    assert early[0] == dt.date(2026, 1, 1)  # never before backfill_from
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the due logic**

`jamstockex/refresh.py`:

```python
"""When a refresh is due, and one refresh run: fetch, parse, merge.

Times are compared in America/Jamaica (config.JAMAICA). `latest_close(now)` is
the most recent weekday market close at or before `now`; its date is the trade
date requested. Each dataset is fetched at most once per close: quotes (with
indices) when `quotes.fetched_at` is before the latest close, and corporate
actions likewise on their own `fetched_at`. A day without trading (the site's
"no data" page) is a successful fetch with zero rows; store.merge marks quotes
stale after 3 of them in a row.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import datetime as dt
import logging
from dataclasses import dataclass, field

from jamstockex import store
from jamstockex.config import JAMAICA, Config
from jamstockex.fetch import FetchError
from jamstockex.models import Quote
from jamstockex.parse import ParseError, parse_corporate_actions, parse_listed_companies, parse_trade_quotes

log = logging.getLogger(__name__)

QUOTES_PATH = "/trading/trade-quotes/?market={market}&date={date}"
LISTINGS_PATH = "/listings/listed-companies/?market={market}"
ACTIONS_PATH = "/trading/corporate-actions/?instrumentCode=all-stocks&fromDate={from_date}&thruDate={thru_date}"


def latest_close(now: dt.datetime, close_time: dt.time = dt.time(17, 30)) -> dt.datetime:
    """The most recent weekday D whose `D close_time` (Jamaica) is <= now."""
    local = now.astimezone(JAMAICA)
    day = local.date()
    if local.time() < close_time:
        day -= dt.timedelta(days=1)
    while day.weekday() >= 5:  # Saturday, Sunday
        day -= dt.timedelta(days=1)
    return dt.datetime.combine(day, close_time, tzinfo=JAMAICA)


def next_close_after(close: dt.datetime, close_time: dt.time = dt.time(17, 30)) -> dt.datetime:
    day = close.astimezone(JAMAICA).date() + dt.timedelta(days=1)
    while day.weekday() >= 5:
        day += dt.timedelta(days=1)
    return dt.datetime.combine(day, close_time, tzinfo=JAMAICA)


def listings_due(state: store.State, quotes: dict[str, Quote] | None, markets: dict[int, str],
                 today: dt.date, every_days: int, force: bool) -> tuple[list[int], str | None]:
    """Which listing markets to fetch, and why. ([], None) means none."""
    everything = list(markets)
    if force:
        return everything, "requested"
    if not state.companies:
        return everything, "no stored listings"
    # Codes a full refresh already failed to explain (state.unmatched) don't trigger another one.
    unknown = set(quotes or {}) - set(state.companies) - state.unmatched
    if unknown:
        return everything, f"{len(unknown)} unknown instrument(s) in quotes"

    def status(label: str) -> store.DatasetStatus:
        return state.markets.get(label, store.DatasetStatus())

    retry = [code for code, label in markets.items() if status(label).stale]
    aged = [
        code for code, label in markets.items()
        if code not in retry and (status(label).as_of is None or (today - status(label).as_of).days >= every_days)
    ]
    reasons = []
    if aged:
        oldest = min((status(markets[c]).as_of for c in aged if status(markets[c]).as_of), default=None)
        reasons.append(f"last refresh {oldest.isoformat() if oldest else 'never'}")
    if retry:
        reasons.append("retrying failed market(s): " + ", ".join(markets[c] for c in retry))
    due_codes = [code for code in markets if code in retry or code in aged]
    return due_codes, "; ".join(reasons) or None


def actions_window(status: store.DatasetStatus, today: dt.date, settings: Config) -> tuple[dt.date, dt.date]:
    """Corporate-actions query range.

    Backfill from `backfill_from` until the first success. After that, look back
    `corporate_actions_window` days from the last success, so an outage of any length is
    caught up on the next good run. Always reaches `corporate_actions_forward` days ahead.
    """
    backfill = settings.refresh.backfill_from
    thru = today + dt.timedelta(days=settings.refresh.corporate_actions_forward)
    if status.as_of is None:
        return backfill, thru
    return max(backfill, status.as_of - dt.timedelta(days=settings.refresh.corporate_actions_window)), thru


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Due:
    close: dt.datetime                  # latest_close(now)
    today: dt.date                      # today in Jamaica
    quotes: bool                        # quotes and indices
    corporate_actions: bool
    listings: tuple[int, ...]           # market codes due before this run's quotes are known
    listings_reason: str | None
    not_before: dt.datetime | None      # automatic attempts wait until then (failure wait, block)

    @property
    def trade_date(self) -> dt.date:
        return self.close.date()

    @property
    def anything(self) -> bool:
        return self.quotes or self.corporate_actions or bool(self.listings)

    def automatic_allowed(self, now: dt.datetime) -> bool:
        return self.anything and (self.not_before is None or now >= self.not_before)


def due(state: store.State, now: dt.datetime, settings: Config, *, force_listings: bool = False) -> Due:
    close_time = settings.refresh.close_time
    close = latest_close(now, close_time)
    today = now.astimezone(JAMAICA).date()

    def before_close(name: str) -> bool:
        fetched = state.status[name].fetched_at
        return fetched is None or fetched < close

    codes, reason = listings_due(state, None, settings.markets, today, settings.refresh.listings_every_days,
                                 force_listings)
    waits = []
    if state.blocked_close is not None and close <= state.blocked_close:
        waits.append(next_close_after(state.blocked_close, close_time))
    if state.last_failure_at is not None:
        retry_at = state.last_failure_at + dt.timedelta(minutes=settings.refresh.retry_after_failure_minutes)
        if now < retry_at:
            waits.append(retry_at)
    return Due(
        close=close,
        today=today,
        quotes=before_close("quotes"),
        corporate_actions=before_close("corporate_actions"),
        listings=tuple(codes),
        listings_reason=reason,
        not_before=max(waits) if waits else None,
    )
```

### Task 13: Refresh: one refresh run and its report

**Why:** runs what is due: quotes first (so an unknown instrument can trigger a listings refresh in the same run), then the due listings markets, then corporate actions. Each dataset fails independently, and stored data is kept. After a block, the fetcher refuses every later request, so every not-yet-fetched due dataset is marked stale, `blocked_close` is recorded, and no automatic attempt happens until the next close (spec §6.2–§6.3, superseded spec §6.1). Parser warnings (missing optional columns, skipped rows) and merge warnings go into the `Report`, which counts requests, per-dataset outcomes (`updated`, `stale`, `skipped`) and companies added, updated and delisted, and actions added. A "no data" quotes page is a successful fetch with zero quotes; three trading days of them in a row put Task 11's no-data warning in the report and mark quotes and indices stale, but the exit code stays 0, no failure wait starts, and the next close is fetched as usual. `tests/fakes.py` serves the synthetic fixtures by path prefix; no test touches the network.

**Files:**
- Modify: `jamstockex/refresh.py` (append)
- Test: `tests/fakes.py`, `tests/test_refresh_run.py`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: from `jamstockex/refresh.py` (Task 12): `due`, `listings_due`, `actions_window`, the `*_PATH` constants; `jamstockex.store.ScrapeResult`, `MergeStats`, `merge`, `State` (Tasks 10–11); `jamstockex.fetch.FetchError`, `BlockedError` (Task 4); the page parsers (Tasks 7–9).
- Produces:
  - `BLOCKED_MESSAGE = "The JSE site is blocking automated requests from this computer. Your stored data is still served; automatic refresh will try again after the next market close."`
  - `@dataclass Report(trade_date: date, requests: int = 0, datasets: dict[str, str] = {}, errors: dict[str, str] = {}, warnings: list[str] = [], listings_reason: str | None = None, stats: MergeStats = MergeStats(), counts: dict[str, int] = {}, blocked: bool = False)` (mutable defaults via `default_factory`), with properties `attempted -> bool` and `exit_code -> int` (1 if any error), and `text() -> str` (the printed report; ends with `BLOCKED_MESSAGE` when blocked, or says `Nothing is due: the stored data is up to date.`)
  - `run_refresh(state: State, fetcher, now: datetime, settings: Config, force_listings: bool = False) -> Report`. It mutates `state`; the caller saves it. `fetcher` is a `Fetcher` or any object with `get(path) -> str`, `request_count: int` and `blocked: bool`.
  - `tests.fakes`: `FakeFetcher(pages: dict[str, str | Exception])` (prefix-matched; a `BlockedError` page sets `blocked`; records `paths`), and `pages(overrides: dict | None = None) -> dict[str, str | Exception]` (the synthetic site: Main Market has `9001` (ACME) and `9002` (BETA), and the other markets are empty)

`tests/fakes.py`:

```python
"""Test doubles shared by the refresh, CLI and server tests."""

from jamstockex.fetch import BlockedError
from tests.helpers import fixture_html


class FakeFetcher:
    """Serves pages by path prefix; mimics Fetcher's block behaviour and request count."""

    def __init__(self, pages):
        self.pages = pages
        self.paths = []
        self.request_count = 0
        self.blocked = False

    def get(self, path):
        if self.blocked:
            raise BlockedError(f"Not requesting {path}: an earlier request was blocked")
        self.paths.append(path)
        self.request_count += 1
        for prefix, page in self.pages.items():
            if path.startswith(prefix):
                if isinstance(page, BlockedError):
                    self.blocked = True
                if isinstance(page, Exception):
                    raise page
                return page
        raise AssertionError(f"unexpected request: {path}")


def pages(overrides=None):
    """The synthetic site: Main Market has two instruments, the other markets are empty."""
    empty = fixture_html("listed-companies-empty.html")
    result = {
        "/trading/trade-quotes/": fixture_html("trade-quotes.html"),
        "/listings/listed-companies/?market=31": fixture_html("listed-companies.html"),
        "/listings/listed-companies/?market=22": empty,
        "/listings/listed-companies/?market=51": empty,
        "/listings/listed-companies/?market=49": empty,
        "/trading/corporate-actions/": fixture_html("corporate-actions.html"),
    }
    result.update(overrides or {})
    return result
```
