### Task 17: Real-page parser checks

**Why:** this task confirms the parsers against the real pages the user saved in Task 1 (superseded spec §9; spec §9). The checks are: every alias matches on every page, no page is a block page or paginated, the holiday page (requested for 2026-10-19) parses as a day without trading (no tables, the site's "no data" message, `({}, [])`) while the trading-day page (2026-11-10) has no such message, each listings page names and selects its configured market, and the numeric `instrument_code`s on the quotes and corporate-actions pages line up with the listing codes. Each test skips when its page is absent, so CI and fresh clones skip all 27. Task 1's findings are already built into Tasks 7–9: the synthetic fixtures copy the real layouts and header text, and every real header normalises to an existing alias, so no alias change is expected. If a header has changed since, Step 4 fixes the aliases and the fixtures, so the committed tests reflect the real layout. No JSE content is committed.

**Files:**
- Test: `tests/test_parse_real.py`
- Possibly modify: the alias tuples in `jamstockex/parse.py`, and the `<th>` text in `tests/fixtures/*.html` and in the tests that `.replace()` it

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `tests/fixtures/real/{listed-companies-31,listed-companies-22,listed-companies-51,listed-companies-49,trade-quotes-trading-day,trade-quotes-holiday,corporate-actions}.html` (Task 1); `LISTING_COLUMNS`, `QUOTE_COLUMNS`, `INDEX_COLUMNS`, `ACTION_COLUMNS`, `matching_tables` and the three page parsers (Tasks 6–9); `extract_tables` (Task 5); `is_block_page` (Task 4); `default_config` (Task 2); `tests.helpers.FIXTURES` (Task 2).
- Produces: nothing new for later tasks.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the real-page tests**

`tests/test_parse_real.py`:

```python
"""Checks against real JSE pages saved by the user in tests/fixtures/real/ (gitignored, never committed).

Every test skips when its page is absent, so CI and fresh clones skip them all.
The file names are the ones Task 1 of the implementation plan asks the user to save.
"""

import datetime as dt
import logging
import re

import pytest

from jamstockex.config import default_config
from jamstockex.fetch import is_block_page
from jamstockex.html import extract_tables
from jamstockex.parse import (
    ACTION_COLUMNS, INDEX_COLUMNS, LISTING_COLUMNS, QUOTE_COLUMNS, is_no_data_page, matching_tables,
    parse_corporate_actions, parse_listed_companies, parse_trade_quotes,
)
from tests.helpers import FIXTURES

REAL = FIXTURES / "real"
MARKETS = default_config().markets  # market code -> label
TRADING_DAY = "trade-quotes-trading-day.html"  # saved for 2026-11-10
HOLIDAY = "trade-quotes-holiday.html"  # requested for 2026-10-19 (National Heroes Day)
ACTIONS = "corporate-actions.html"
PAGES = [f"listed-companies-{code}.html" for code in MARKETS] + [TRADING_DAY, HOLIDAY, ACTIONS]
PAGINATION = re.compile(r"[?&](page|paged|offset|start)=\d|class=\"[^\"]*pagination|rel=\"next\"", re.IGNORECASE)
NOT_FOUND = "column(s) not found"
SELECTED_MARKET = re.compile(r'id="selectedMarket"[^>]*>\s*([^<]*?)\s*<')


def real(name: str) -> str:
    path = REAL / name
    if not path.exists():
        pytest.skip(f"real page {name} not saved in tests/fixtures/real/")
    return path.read_text(encoding="utf-8", errors="replace")


@pytest.mark.parametrize("name", PAGES)
def test_real_page_is_not_a_block_page_and_not_paginated(name):
    html = real(name)
    assert not is_block_page(html), f"{name} is a CloudFront block page; save it again from a browser"
    assert not PAGINATION.search(html), f"{name} looks paginated: STOP and tell the user"


@pytest.mark.parametrize("code, label", MARKETS.items())
def test_real_listed_companies(code, label, caplog):
    with caplog.at_level(logging.WARNING, logger="jamstockex.parse"):
        companies = parse_listed_companies(real(f"listed-companies-{code}.html"), label)
    assert companies, f"{label}: no companies parsed"
    assert all(c.instrument_code and c.symbol and c.name for c in companies)
    assert not [m for m in caplog.messages if NOT_FOUND in m], caplog.messages


@pytest.mark.parametrize("code, label", MARKETS.items())
def test_real_market_label_appears_on_its_page(code, label):
    """Catches a market code that points at a differently named market.

    Every listings page names all markets in its dropdown, so the selected market is checked when
    the page marks it.
    """
    page = real(f"listed-companies-{code}.html")
    assert label.lower() in page.lower(), f"market {code}: the page never mentions {label!r}"
    selected = SELECTED_MARKET.findall(page)
    if selected:
        assert [s.lower() for s in selected] == [label.lower()], f"market {code}: the page selects {selected}"


def test_real_trade_quotes(caplog):
    with caplog.at_level(logging.WARNING, logger="jamstockex.parse"):
        quotes, indices = parse_trade_quotes(real(TRADING_DAY), dt.date(2026, 11, 10))
    assert quotes, "no quotes parsed from the trading-day page"
    assert indices, "no indices parsed from the trading-day page"
    assert sum(q.close is not None for q in quotes.values()) >= 0.9 * len(quotes)
    assert not [m for m in caplog.messages if NOT_FOUND in m], caplog.messages


def test_real_holiday_page_is_a_day_without_trading():
    """The site answers a date without trading with no tables and its "no data" message. A ParseError
    here means that message changed; quotes would mean it sent another session's data (STOP)."""
    html = real(HOLIDAY)
    assert is_no_data_page(html), "the holiday page no longer has the site's 'no data' message"
    assert not matching_tables(extract_tables(html), QUOTE_COLUMNS, ("symbol", "close"))
    assert parse_trade_quotes(html, dt.date(2026, 10, 19)) == ({}, [])


def test_real_trading_day_page_has_no_no_data_message():
    assert not is_no_data_page(real(TRADING_DAY))


def test_real_corporate_actions(caplog):
    with caplog.at_level(logging.WARNING, logger="jamstockex.parse"):
        actions = parse_corporate_actions(real(ACTIONS))
    assert actions, "no corporate actions parsed"
    assert all(re.fullmatch(r"[a-z0-9_]+", a.type) for a in actions)
    dividends = [a for a in actions if a.type == "dividend"]
    assert dividends, f"no dividends; types seen: {sorted({a.type for a in actions})}"
    assert sum(a.amount is not None for a in dividends) >= 0.9 * len(dividends)
    assert not [m for m in caplog.messages if NOT_FOUND in m], caplog.messages


@pytest.mark.parametrize("name, aliases, required", [
    *[(f"listed-companies-{code}.html", LISTING_COLUMNS, ("name", "symbol")) for code in MARKETS],
    (TRADING_DAY, QUOTE_COLUMNS, ("symbol", "close")),
    (TRADING_DAY, INDEX_COLUMNS, ("name", "value")),
    (ACTIONS, ACTION_COLUMNS, ("symbol", "type", "amount")),
])
def test_real_every_alias_table_matches(name, aliases, required):
    """Lists the fields whose header matched no alias, so the fix is obvious."""
    matches = matching_tables(extract_tables(real(name)), aliases, required)
    assert matches, f"{name}: no table has the required columns {required}"
    unresolved = {f for _, cols in matches for f, idx in cols.items() if idx is None}
    if aliases is QUOTE_COLUMNS:
        unresolved.discard("change_pct")  # computed when the page has no % column
    assert unresolved == set(), f"{name}: add the real header names to the aliases for {sorted(unresolved)}"


def test_real_quote_and_action_codes_match_listing_codes():
    """instrument_code must have one format on every page (a numeric ID), or quotes and
    actions never reach their company."""
    listed = {
        c.instrument_code
        for code, label in MARKETS.items()
        for c in parse_listed_companies(real(f"listed-companies-{code}.html"), label)
    }
    quotes, _ = parse_trade_quotes(real(TRADING_DAY), dt.date(2026, 11, 10))
    actions = {a.instrument_code for a in parse_corporate_actions(real(ACTIONS))}
    assert None not in actions, "a corporate-actions row has no ?instrument= link"
    assert all(code.isdigit() for code in listed | set(quotes) | actions), "instrument_code is no longer numeric"
    for name, codes in (("quote", set(quotes)), ("corporate-action", actions)):
        unknown = sorted(codes - listed)
        assert len(unknown) <= len(codes) // 10, f"{name} codes missing from every listings page: {unknown[:20]}"
```

Run: `.venv/bin/pytest tests/test_parse_real.py -v`
Expected: `27 passed`. If any fail, go to Step 4; otherwise go to Step 5.

**Stop conditions. Report to the user before changing any code:**
- `test_real_page_is_not_a_block_page_and_not_paginated` fails on pagination: the app reads one page per request.
- `test_real_holiday_page_is_a_day_without_trading` fails because quotes came back (the site answers a holiday with the previous session's data), or because the "no data" message changed (then `NO_DATA_MARKER` in `jamstockex/parse.py` and the holiday fixture need the new text, after the user confirms it).
- `test_real_corporate_actions` or the corporate-actions alias test fails because there is no action-type column.
- `test_real_market_label_appears_on_its_page` fails, and the user confirms that the market code shows a different market.
- `test_real_quote_and_action_codes_match_listing_codes` fails: the `instrument_code` formats differ between pages, are no longer numeric, or corporate-actions rows lost their `?instrument=` link.

- [ ] **Step 4: If an alias test fails, fix the aliases and the synthetic fixtures**

Print the normalised headers of every real table:

```bash
.venv/bin/python - <<'EOF'
from pathlib import Path
from jamstockex.html import extract_tables
from jamstockex.parse import data_rows, header_index, row_instrument_code
for path in sorted(Path("tests/fixtures/real").glob("*.html")):
    print(f"== {path.name}")
    for n, table in enumerate(extract_tables(path.read_text(encoding="utf-8", errors="replace"))):
        rows = data_rows(table)
        code = next((c for c in map(row_instrument_code, rows) if c), None)
        print(f"  table {n}: rows={len(rows)} headers={list(header_index(table))} sample_code={code!r}")
EOF
```

For each field that the failing test names, add the printed normalised header to that field's alias tuple in `jamstockex/parse.py` (`LISTING_COLUMNS`, `QUOTE_COLUMNS`, `INDEX_COLUMNS` or `ACTION_COLUMNS`). Put it **first** in the tuple when it should win over an existing alias. Then change the matching `<th>` text in the synthetic fixture (`tests/fixtures/listed-companies*.html`, `trade-quotes*.html` or `corporate-actions.html`), plus any `.replace("<th>…</th>", …)` in `tests/test_parse_*.py` and `tests/test_refresh_run.py` that targets the old text. That way the committed tests use the real layout. If the corporate-actions symbol cell holds more than the symbol (for example a company name), stop and report it to the user rather than guessing.

Re-run `.venv/bin/pytest -q` until everything passes.

### Task 18: CI and release workflows

**Why:** `ci.yml` runs the offline suite on Python 3.11 and 3.12 for pushes to `main`/`develop` and PRs into them. `release.yml` runs on a `v*` tag (spec §7):
- **Binaries:** PyInstaller 6.10.0 (build time only, Python 3.12) builds one-file executables on `windows-latest`, `macos-14`, `macos-15-intel` and `ubuntu-22.04`, bundling `openapi.json` and `static/` with `--add-data`.
- **`.pyz`:** `.github/build_pyz.py` builds `jamstockex.pyz` with the standard library's `zipapp` (shebang `/usr/bin/env python3`).
- **Smoke test:** every artifact is smoke-tested on its own OS by `.github/smoke_test.py`. It starts the artifact with `--offline --data-dir <temp> --port 8765`, waits for the port, GETs `/v2/status`, `/docs` and `/openapi.json` on 127.0.0.1, expects 200 and stops it. The `.pyz` is smoke-tested on Python 3.11. Nothing contacts the JSE site.
- **Publish:** every artifact and a `SHA256SUMS` file go to the GitHub Release.

The helper scripts are tested locally against the source tree. The PyInstaller command was checked on macOS arm64 while planning: it built, and the smoke test passed.

**Files:**
- Create: `.github/build_pyz.py`, `.github/smoke_test.py`, `.github/pyinstaller_entry.py`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `.github/workflows/release.yml`
- Test: `tests/test_release_scripts.py`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `jamstockex.__main__.main` (Task 16); the packaged files (Task 14); `tests.helpers.ROOT` (Task 2).
- Produces:
  - `.github/build_pyz.py <output.pyz>`: `build(target: Path) -> Path`; the archive's `__main__.py` passes `main()`'s return value to `sys.exit`, so exit codes survive
  - `.github/smoke_test.py [--port 8765] [--timeout 90] -- <command…>`: exits 0 and prints `smoke test passed`, or exits non-zero with `FAIL: …`
  - `.github/pyinstaller_entry.py`: the PyInstaller entry script
  - Release asset names: `jamstockex-windows-x64.exe`, `jamstockex-macos-arm64`, `jamstockex-macos-x64`, `jamstockex-linux-x64`, `jamstockex.pyz`, `SHA256SUMS`

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests**

`tests/test_release_scripts.py`:

```python
"""The release helpers in .github/, run locally against the source tree (offline)."""

import socket
import subprocess
import sys
import zipfile

from tests.helpers import ROOT


def free_port():
    with socket.socket() as sock:
        sock.bind(("127.0.0.1", 0))
        return sock.getsockname()[1]


def smoke(*command):
    return subprocess.run(
        [sys.executable, str(ROOT / ".github" / "smoke_test.py"), "--port", str(free_port()), "--timeout", "30",
         "--", *command],
        capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=90,
    )


def test_smoke_test_passes_against_the_source_tree():
    result = smoke(sys.executable, "-m", "jamstockex")
    assert result.returncode == 0, result.stdout + result.stderr
    assert "/v2/status -> 200" in result.stdout
    assert "/docs -> 200" in result.stdout
    assert "/openapi.json -> 200" in result.stdout


def test_smoke_test_fails_when_the_app_exits_early():
    result = smoke(sys.executable, "-c", "import sys; sys.exit(3)")
    assert result.returncode != 0
    assert "exited early with code 3" in result.stdout + result.stderr


def test_pyz_builds_runs_and_keeps_exit_codes(tmp_path):
    target = tmp_path / "jamstockex.pyz"
    subprocess.run([sys.executable, str(ROOT / ".github" / "build_pyz.py"), str(target)], check=True,
                   capture_output=True)
    names = zipfile.ZipFile(target).namelist()
    assert "__main__.py" in names
    assert "jamstockex/openapi.json" in names
    assert "jamstockex/static/swagger/swagger-ui-bundle.js" in names
    assert not [n for n in names if "__pycache__" in n]
    assert target.read_bytes().startswith(b"#!/usr/bin/env python3\n")

    version = subprocess.run([sys.executable, str(target), "--version"], capture_output=True, text=True)
    assert version.returncode == 0 and version.stdout.strip() == "jamstockex 2.0.0"

    (tmp_path / "data").mkdir()
    (tmp_path / "data" / "config.toml").write_text("[server]\nport = 0\n", encoding="utf-8")
    bad = subprocess.run([sys.executable, str(target), "refresh", "--data-dir", str(tmp_path / "data")],
                         capture_output=True, text=True)
    assert bad.returncode == 2  # exit codes survive the zipapp entry point

    result = smoke(sys.executable, str(target))
    assert result.returncode == 0, result.stdout + result.stderr
```

`.github/build_pyz.py`:

```python
"""Build jamstockex.pyz with the standard library's zipapp.

Usage: python .github/build_pyz.py dist/jamstockex.pyz

The archive holds the jamstockex package (with openapi.json and static/) and a
__main__.py that passes main()'s return value to sys.exit, so exit codes survive.
"""

import shutil
import sys
import tempfile
import zipapp
from pathlib import Path

ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent
MAIN = "import sys\n\nfrom jamstockex.__main__ import main\n\nsys.exit(main())\n"


def build(target: Path) -> Path:
    target.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as tmp:
        staging = Path(tmp) / "app"
        shutil.copytree(ROOT / "jamstockex", staging / "jamstockex",
                        ignore=shutil.ignore_patterns("__pycache__", "*.pyc"))
        (staging / "__main__.py").write_text(MAIN, encoding="utf-8")
        zipapp.create_archive(staging, target, interpreter="/usr/bin/env python3", compressed=True)
    return target


if __name__ == "__main__":
    if len(sys.argv) != 2:
        sys.exit("usage: python .github/build_pyz.py <output.pyz>")
    print(build(Path(sys.argv[1])))
```

`.github/smoke_test.py`:

```python
"""Start a built artifact offline, check three URLs on 127.0.0.1, stop it.

Usage: python .github/smoke_test.py [--port 8765] [--timeout 90] -- <command...>
e.g.   python .github/smoke_test.py -- dist/jamstockex-linux-x64
       python .github/smoke_test.py -- python dist/jamstockex.pyz

Never contacts the JSE: the artifact runs with --offline and an empty temp data folder.
"""

import argparse
import socket
import subprocess
import sys
import tempfile
import time
import urllib.request

PATHS = ("/v2/status", "/docs", "/openapi.json")


def wait_for_port(port: int, process: subprocess.Popen, timeout: float) -> None:
    deadline = time.monotonic() + timeout
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        if process.poll() is not None:
            raise SystemExit(f"FAIL: the app exited early with code {process.returncode}")
        try:
            with socket.create_connection(("127.0.0.1", port), timeout=1):
                return
        except OSError:
            time.sleep(0.5)
    raise SystemExit(f"FAIL: nothing listening on 127.0.0.1:{port} after {timeout:.0f}s")


def main(argv=None) -> int:
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser()
    parser.add_argument("--port", type=int, default=8765)
    parser.add_argument("--timeout", type=float, default=90)
    parser.add_argument("command", nargs=argparse.REMAINDER)
    args = parser.parse_args(argv)
    command = args.command[1:] if args.command[:1] == ["--"] else args.command
    if not command:
        parser.error("give the command to start after --")
    with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as data_dir:
        process = subprocess.Popen([*command, "--offline", "--data-dir", data_dir, "--port", str(args.port)])
        try:
            wait_for_port(args.port, process, args.timeout)
            for path in PATHS:
                with urllib.request.urlopen(f"http://127.0.0.1:{args.port}{path}", timeout=10) as response:
                    print(f"{path} -> {response.status}")
                    if response.status != 200:
                        raise SystemExit(f"FAIL: {path} returned {response.status}")
        finally:
            process.terminate()
            try:
                process.wait(timeout=15)
            except subprocess.TimeoutExpired:
                process.kill()
                process.wait()
    print("smoke test passed")
    return 0


if __name__ == "__main__":
    sys.exit(main())
```

`.github/pyinstaller_entry.py`:

```python
"""PyInstaller entry point: run the CLI and pass its exit code to the OS."""

import sys

from jamstockex.__main__ import main

sys.exit(main())
```

`.github/workflows/ci.yml`:

```yaml
name: ci

on:
  push:
    branches: [main, develop]
  pull_request:
    branches: [main, develop]

permissions:
  contents: read

jobs:
  test:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    strategy:
      fail-fast: false
      matrix:
        python-version: ["3.11", "3.12"]
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v4
      - uses: actions/setup-python@v5
        with:
          python-version: ${{ matrix.python-version }}
          cache: pip
          cache-dependency-path: requirements-dev.txt
      - run: pip install -r requirements-dev.txt
      - run: pytest -q
```

`.github/workflows/release.yml`:

```yaml
name: release

on:
  push:
    tags: ["v*"]

permissions:
  contents: read

jobs:
  binary:
    name: binary (${{ matrix.asset }})
    runs-on: ${{ matrix.os }}
    strategy:
      fail-fast: false
      matrix:
        include:
          - { os: windows-latest, asset: jamstockex-windows-x64.exe, name: jamstockex-windows-x64 }
          - { os: macos-14, asset: jamstockex-macos-arm64, name: jamstockex-macos-arm64 }
          - { os: macos-15-intel, asset: jamstockex-macos-x64, name: jamstockex-macos-x64 }
          - { os: ubuntu-22.04, asset: jamstockex-linux-x64, name: jamstockex-linux-x64 }
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v4
      - uses: actions/setup-python@v5
        with:
          python-version: "3.12"
      - name: Install PyInstaller (build time only)
        run: pip install pyinstaller==6.10.0
      - name: Build one-file executable
        shell: bash
        run: >
          pyinstaller --onefile --clean --noconfirm --name "${{ matrix.name }}" --paths .
          --add-data "jamstockex/openapi.json:jamstockex"
          --add-data "jamstockex/static:jamstockex/static"
          .github/pyinstaller_entry.py
      - name: Smoke test (offline, 127.0.0.1 only)
        shell: bash
        run: python .github/smoke_test.py --port 8765 -- "dist/${{ matrix.asset }}"
      - uses: actions/upload-artifact@v4
        with:
          name: ${{ matrix.asset }}
          path: dist/${{ matrix.asset }}
          if-no-files-found: error

  pyz:
    runs-on: ubuntu-22.04
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v4
      - uses: actions/setup-python@v5
        with:
          python-version: "3.11"
      - name: Build jamstockex.pyz
        run: python .github/build_pyz.py dist/jamstockex.pyz
      - name: Smoke test on Python 3.11 (offline, 127.0.0.1 only)
        run: python .github/smoke_test.py --port 8765 -- python dist/jamstockex.pyz
      - uses: actions/upload-artifact@v4
        with:
          name: jamstockex.pyz
          path: dist/jamstockex.pyz
          if-no-files-found: error

  publish:
    needs: [binary, pyz]
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    permissions:
      contents: write
    steps:
      - uses: actions/download-artifact@v4
        with:
          path: artifacts
          merge-multiple: true
      - name: Write SHA256SUMS
        working-directory: artifacts
        run: |
          sha256sum jamstockex-* jamstockex.pyz > SHA256SUMS
          cat SHA256SUMS
      - name: Create the GitHub Release
        env:
          GH_TOKEN: ${{ github.token }}
          GH_REPO: ${{ github.repository }}
        run: >
          gh release create "$GITHUB_REF_NAME" artifacts/*
          --title "$GITHUB_REF_NAME"
          --notes "Local-first JSE end-of-day data app. Personal, noncommercial use only. Not financial advice. See README.md for first-run steps and SHA256SUMS to verify downloads."
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Replace `README.md`**

````markdown
# jamstockex

A small app that keeps **Jamaica Stock Exchange (JSE) end-of-day data** in a file on your computer and
serves it to you as a JSON API with Swagger docs, at `http://127.0.0.1:8765`. Only your own computer can
reach it.

> **Not affiliated with or endorsed by the Jamaica Stock Exchange.** The data belongs to the JSE Group.
> Personal, noncommercial use only. Not financial advice. See [License and data](#license-and-data).

## Download and first run

Download the file for your computer from the latest GitHub Release, plus `SHA256SUMS` if you want to check it:

| System | File |
|---|---|
| Windows (64-bit) | `jamstockex-windows-x64.exe` |
| macOS, Apple Silicon | `jamstockex-macos-arm64` |
| macOS, Intel | `jamstockex-macos-x64` |
| Linux (x86-64) | `jamstockex-linux-x64` |
| Any system with Python 3.11+ | `jamstockex.pyz` (run `python3 jamstockex.pyz`) |

The binaries are not code-signed, so your system asks you to approve them once:
- **macOS:** `chmod +x jamstockex-macos-*`, run it once, then open System Settings → Privacy & Security and
  click **Open Anyway**.
- **Linux:** `chmod +x jamstockex-linux-x64`, then run `./jamstockex-linux-x64`.
- **Windows:** when SmartScreen appears, click **More info → Run anyway**.

When it starts you'll see:

```
JSE data API running at http://127.0.0.1:8765/v2/   docs: http://127.0.0.1:8765/docs
Data: <data folder>/jse-data.json (as of 2026-11-09)
Refreshing in background… (Ctrl+C to stop)
```

## Using the API

Open `http://127.0.0.1:8765/docs` for interactive docs. Everything is read-only GET:

| Path | Returns |
|---|---|
| `/v2/status` | Freshness of each dataset (`as_of`, `fetched_at`, `stale`, `last_error`), `blocked`, `next_check_at`, `data_file` |
| `/v2/stocks` | Every instrument with its latest quote. Filters: `symbol`, `market`, `listed=true/false` |
| `/v2/stocks/{instrument_code}` | One instrument with its `dividends` and `corporate_actions` |
| `/v2/indices` | Market indices |
| `/v2/corporate-actions` | All corporate actions. Filters: `symbol`, `type` |
| `/v2/dividends` | Dividends. Filters: `symbol`, `upcoming=true/false` |

```shell
curl -s "http://127.0.0.1:8765/v2/stocks?market=Main%20Market" | python3 -m json.tool | head -40
```

Every response has a `meta` block (`as_of`, `saved_at`, `stale`, source, notice, license) and a `data` block.
Instruments are keyed by `instrument_code`: a company listed in JMD and USD has one symbol and two instruments.
Unknown values are `null`, never `0`. Dates are `YYYY-MM-DD` and timestamps are UTC.

## Refresh, and what "blocked" means

The app fetches new data at most once per trading day, after the 17:30 Jamaica-time close, and only when
the stored data is out of date. A typical day needs 2 requests to the JSE site; listed companies are
refreshed weekly. It uses one honest User-Agent and waits between requests.

- `jamstockex refresh` runs one refresh now and prints a report (`--listings` also refreshes every market).
- `jamstockex --offline` serves the stored data without refreshing.

On a day without trading (a public holiday) the JSE site has no trade data, and the previous quotes are kept.
If it reports no trade data for 3 trading days in a row, `/v2/status` marks quotes and indices stale, because
the site may have changed; the app still tries again after each close.

If the JSE site blocks automated requests from your connection, the app says so plainly:

> The JSE site is blocking automated requests from this computer. Your stored data is still served;
> automatic refresh will try again after the next market close.

`/v2/status` then shows `"blocked": true`. The app never retries with a different identity or works around a
block. On a first run with no stored data, there is nothing to serve until a refresh gets through.

## Configuration

Data lives in your per-user data folder (change it with `--data-dir`):
- **macOS:** `~/Library/Application Support/jamstockex/`
- **Windows:** `%APPDATA%\jamstockex\`
- **Linux:** `$XDG_DATA_HOME/jamstockex/`, or `~/.local/share/jamstockex/`

No configuration is needed. To change a setting, create `config.toml` in that folder with only the keys you
want to override. For example:

```toml
[refresh]
check_every_minutes = 30

[server]
port = 8766
```

The sections are `[source]`, `[markets]`, `[quotes]`, `[refresh]` and `[server]`; the defaults are in
`jamstockex/config.py`. Unknown keys and invalid values stop the app with a message naming the key.
`--port <n>` overrides the port for one run. If the port is taken, the app exits and suggests another one.

Exit codes: `0` ok; `1` a dataset failed or was blocked, or another refresh is running; `2` a config,
data-file or port problem.

## Development

```shell
python3.12 -m venv .venv && .venv/bin/pip install -r requirements-dev.txt
.venv/bin/pytest -q              # offline; never contacts the JSE site
.venv/bin/python -m jamstockex --offline
```

The app uses only the Python standard library (3.11+). Releases are built by `.github/workflows/release.yml`
when a `v*` tag is pushed.

## License and data

- **Code:** [PolyForm Noncommercial 1.0.0](LICENSE). Copyright (c) 2022-2026 Stephen Clarke. It is free for
  noncommercial use; commercial use needs a separate license, so open an issue to ask. Versions up to `v1.0`
  were MIT and remain so. See [NOTICE](NOTICE).
- **Data:** the property of Jamaica Stock Exchange Limited / the JSE Group, and not covered by the code license.
- **Use:** intended for local, personal use on your own computer, not for hosting as a service or
  republishing the data.
- **Not financial advice.** The data may be late, incomplete or wrong.
- **Third-party:** Swagger UI (Apache-2.0), in `jamstockex/static/swagger/`.
````

- [ ] **Step 4: Replace `CLAUDE.md`**

````markdown
# CLAUDE.md

This file provides guidance to Claude Code (claude.ai/code) when working with code in this repository.

## Overview

`jamstockex` is a single-user, local-first app. It keeps Jamaica Stock Exchange end-of-day data in a local
`jse-data.json` and serves it read-only on `127.0.0.1` as a JSON API (`/v2/…`) with Swagger docs (`/docs`).
It uses only the Python 3.11+ standard library. Spec: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-10-v2-local-app-design.md`.

## Commands

```shell
/opt/homebrew/bin/python3.12 -m venv --clear .venv && .venv/bin/pip install -r requirements-dev.txt
.venv/bin/pytest -q                                          # offline suite
.venv/bin/pytest tests/test_server.py::test_status -q        # single test
.venv/bin/python -m jamstockex --offline --data-dir /tmp/jse # serve without refreshing
.venv/bin/python -m jamstockex refresh                       # one real refresh (contacts the JSE site)
```

## Architecture

- `config.py`: built-in defaults plus an optional `<data_dir>/config.toml`. `JAMAICA` is a fixed UTC-05:00 offset.
- `fetch.py`: `Fetcher` sends one honest User-Agent, pauses between requests, and retries only 5xx and
  connection errors. On 403/429 or a CloudFront page it raises `BlockedError` and refuses all further requests.
- `html.py`: `extract_tables` on `html.parser` (no BeautifulSoup).
- `parse.py`: columns are matched by **normalised header name** through alias tuples (`LISTING_COLUMNS`,
  `QUOTE_COLUMNS`, `INDEX_COLUMNS`, `ACTION_COLUMNS`). Value parsers return `None`, never 0. A missing
  required table or header raises `ParseError`; the fix is usually a new alias. A trade-quotes page without a
  quotes table is a day without trading only if it has the site's "no data" message (`NO_DATA_MARKER`).
- `store.py`: `State`, `load` and atomic `save` of `jse-data.json`, `RefreshLock`, and `merge` (per-market
  delisting guard, `unmatched_codes`, corporate-action dedupe, symbol → `instrument_code` mapping, and the
  no-data safety net: 3 trading days in a row without quotes mark quotes and indices stale).
- `refresh.py`: `latest_close`/`due` (at most one successful refresh per weekday close, a retry wait after
  failure, and none after a block until the next close), then `run_refresh` → `Report`.
- `server.py`: `Snapshot` and `make_server`. It binds 127.0.0.1 only, checks the Host header, sends no CORS
  headers and serves from `openapi.json` and `static/`. `ROUTES` must match `openapi.json` (a test checks this).
- `__main__.py`: CLI (`serve`, `refresh`, `--version`), data-folder resolution, and `Service` (the background
  refresh thread).
- `.github/`: `build_pyz.py`, `smoke_test.py`, `pyinstaller_entry.py`; `ci.yml` and `release.yml`.

## Rules

- **Standard library only** in `jamstockex/` (`tests/test_stdlib_only.py`). pytest is dev-only.
- **Never rotate or spoof the User-Agent, and never work around a block** (JSE T&C §8.2).
- **No `jamstockex.com` URLs** in API output, `openapi.json`, docs, README or NOTICE (JSE T&C §8.5.3).
  Error messages name request paths, never hosts.
- **Bind 127.0.0.1 only; there is no host option.**
- **Never commit real JSE pages.** `tests/fixtures/real/` is gitignored; committed fixtures are synthetic.
- **Tests stay offline** (127.0.0.1 sockets only).
- **License:** code is PolyForm Noncommercial 1.0.0; the data belongs to the JSE Group (see NOTICE).
````

```bash
rm -rf __pycache__

  | grep -v -E '^(\./)?(\.venv|docs/superpowers)/' || echo "no stale references"
.venv/bin/pytest -q
```

### Task 20: Live check by the user, PR to `main`, tag `v2.0.0`

**Why:** this is the only task in which anything contacts the JSE site, and the user does it from their own computer (spec §9 step 4). Success is the goal. A clear blocked message, with no crash and stored data still served, is the acceptable failure. Either way the outcome is reported to the user. Then v2 lands on `main` and the tag builds the release (spec §9 step 5). **Every outward-facing step needs the user's explicit approval at the time it runs.**

**Files:** none.

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: the whole app (Tasks 2–19).
- Produces: a merged PR, the `v2.0.0` tag, and a GitHub Release with five binaries/archives and `SHA256SUMS`.

- [ ] **Step 1: Final local verification**

```bash
.venv/bin/pytest -q
git status --short          # expect only "?? jamstockex.log" (if present)
git log --oneline main..HEAD
```

Expected: `334 passed` with the real pages present (`307 passed, 27 skipped` without them), and a clean tree.

- [ ] **Step 2: Ask the user to run the live check from their computer**

Send the user this message and wait for their reply:

> Please run one real refresh from your own computer. It makes about 6 requests to the JSE site, a few seconds apart, with the honest User-Agent: `.venv/bin/python -m jamstockex refresh`, then `echo $?` for its exit code. Then start the app with `.venv/bin/python -m jamstockex --offline`, open `http://127.0.0.1:8765/v2/status` and `http://127.0.0.1:8765/docs`, and stop it with Ctrl+C. Please paste the refresh report and the exit code.

Read the report:
- **Exit 0:** success. Check that `/v2/status` shows all four datasets with `"stale": false` and that `/v2/stocks` is non-empty.
- **Exit 1 with the blocked message:** the acceptable failure mode. Confirm that the app didn't crash, that `/v2/status` shows `"blocked": true`, and that `/docs` loads. Tell the user plainly that the JSE site blocks automated requests from their connection, that the app will retry only after the next market close, and that nothing will work around the block. **Never** suggest changing the User-Agent or any other workaround.
- **Exit 1 with a `ParseError`:** go back to Task 17 Step 4 with the page it names.
- **Exit 1 with a `FetchError`** (timeouts, 5xx): report it. A later `jamstockex refresh` retries only the failed datasets.

- [ ] **Step 3: Push the branch and open a PR into `main` (after approval)**

Before running, replace `<result from Task 20 Step 2>` in the body with the actual outcome (for example `ok, exit 0` or `blocked, exit 1, stored data served`).

```bash
git -c credential.helper= -c credential.helper='!gh auth git-credential' push -u origin feat/v2-static-api
gh pr create --base main --head feat/v2-static-api --title "v2: local-first JSE data app" --body "$(cat <<'EOF'
## Summary
- Replaces the MongoDB scraper with `jamstockex`: a stdlib-only Python 3.11+ app that keeps JSE end-of-day data in a local `jse-data.json` and serves a read-only `/v2/` JSON API with Swagger docs on 127.0.0.1 only.
- Refreshes at most once per trading day (typically 2 requests); one honest User-Agent; a block is reported and never worked around.
- Header-matched parsing, null-safe values, per-market delisting guard, stale tracking, corporate-action dedupe.
- Release workflow builds unsigned one-file executables (Windows, macOS arm64/x64, Linux) and a `.pyz`, smoke-tests each offline, and publishes `SHA256SUMS`.
- Relicenses the code (v2+) under PolyForm Noncommercial 1.0.0; NOTICE states the data belongs to the JSE Group. v1.0 stays MIT.

## Test plan
- [ ] `pytest -q` passes in CI on Python 3.11 and 3.12 (offline)
- [ ] Real-page parser checks pass locally (`tests/test_parse_real.py`)
- [ ] Live `jamstockex refresh` from the user's computer: <result from Task 20 Step 2>

Spec: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-10-v2-local-app-design.md`

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)
EOF
)"
gh pr checks --watch
```

Expected: both `ci` checks (3.11 and 3.12) pass. The user reviews and merges the PR; don't merge it without their go-ahead.

- [ ] **Step 4: Tag `v2.0.0` on the merge commit and watch the release (after approval)**

```bash
git fetch origin main
git tag -a v2.0.0 origin/main -m "v2.0.0: local-first JSE data app, PolyForm Noncommercial license"
git -c credential.helper= -c credential.helper='!gh auth git-credential' push origin v2.0.0
gh run list --workflow release.yml --limit 1 --json databaseId,status,headBranch   # repeat until the v2.0.0 run appears
RUN_ID=$(gh run list --workflow release.yml --limit 1 --json databaseId -q '.[0].databaseId')
gh run watch "$RUN_ID" --exit-status
gh release view v2.0.0 --json assets -q '.assets[].name'
```

Expected: the run is green, and the release lists `SHA256SUMS`, `jamstockex-linux-x64`, `jamstockex-macos-arm64`, `jamstockex-macos-x64`, `jamstockex-windows-x64.exe` and `jamstockex.pyz` (without `jamstockex-macos-x64` if Task 18 dropped it). If a job fails, read its log with `gh run view "$RUN_ID" --log-failed` and fix the problem through a new PR. Delete and re-push the tag only with the user's approval.

- [ ] **Step 5: Tell the user the outcome**

Report the live-check result, the PR link, the release link, and the first-run notes from the README: Open Anyway on macOS, More info → Run anyway on Windows, and `chmod +x` on macOS and Linux.

| §3.2 `html.py` | thead/tbody/tfoot order, unclosed cells/rows, nested tables, hrefs per cell, whitespace collapsing | 5 |
| §3.2 `parse.py` (superseded §3.2) | Alias tables, `ParseError(page, problem)`, required quotes table unless the page has the site's "no data" message, indices warn only, one warning per page, skipped-row counts, original case, `None` not 0 | 6–9 |
